### Epic 2.3: PlannerAgent

**Goal**: Implement the single-threaded async coordinator that owns the mailbox dequeue loop, plan lock, cancel set, and lifecycle phases (INIT, SHUTDOWN, CRASH_RECOVERY). PlannerAgent is the entry point for the Planner module -- it creates all services, wires all ports, and drives PipelineController for each plan.

> **Reference**: planner.md §3.1 (Planner Core -- PlannerAgent), §4 (Mailbox & Message Protocol), §23 (Lifecycle -- 6 Phases), §24 (Concurrency Model), §24.1 (V1 Single Plan), §24.2 (Cancellation), §24.3 (Micro-Replan Concurrency), §30.5.1 F02 (planner_agent.py spec).
>
> - **PlannerAgent** is a single-threaded async actor running in one Python event loop. No thread pool, no multiprocessing, no parallel plan execution (§24.1).
> - **V1 concurrency**: one plan at a time via `asyncio.Lock`. Mailbox queues additional requests (FIFO, max depth 5).
> - **Lifecycle phases**: INIT (once at startup), PLAN_START (per plan), STAGE_TRANSITION (per stage boundary -- delegated to PipelineController), PLAN_END (per plan -- delegated to PipelineController), SHUTDOWN (once at stop), CRASH_RECOVERY (once at startup if WAL has in-flight plan).
> - **Cancel protocol** (§24.2): cooperative flag-based, checked between stages. `_cancel_set: Set[str]` managed by PlannerAgent, passed to PipelineController via cancel_check closure.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 2.3.1 | PlannerAgent skeleton & constructor | DONE | k1/planner/planner_agent.py [F02] | **Signature**: `class PlannerAgent` with constructor `__init__(self, mailbox: IMailboxPort, pipeline: PipelineController, event_port: IEventPort, config: PlannerConfig)`. **Internal state**: `_mailbox: IMailboxPort`, `_pipeline: PipelineController`, `_event_port: IEventPort`, `_config: PlannerConfig`, `_plan_lock: asyncio.Lock` (V1 single-plan exclusion), `_cancel_set: Set[str]` (request_ids pending cancellation), `_running: bool` (controls dequeue loop), `_subscriptions: List[Subscription]` (event subscriptions for cleanup at SHUTDOWN). **Public interface**: `async def start() -> None` (runs INIT + CRASH_RECOVERY + enters dequeue loop), `async def stop() -> None` (runs SHUTDOWN), `async def on_cancel(request_id: str) -> None` (adds to _cancel_set, called by MailboxAdapter.send_cancel), `async def micro_replan(request: MicroReplanRequest) -> Optional[CommittedPlan]` (acquires_plan_lock, delegates to pipeline.micro_replan). **Dependency layer**: Layer 3 (§30.6) -- imports Layer 2 (PipelineController), Layer 1 (ports), Layer 0 (types/config). **Gotcha**: PlannerAgent does NOT create services -- that is PlannerFactory's responsibility (M6). PlannerAgent receives fully-wired PipelineController via constructor injection. |
| 2.3.2 | Mailbox dequeue loop | DONE | k1/planner/planner_agent.py [F02] | **Execution model** (§24.1.2): `async def _run_loop(self) -> None`. **Loop body**: `while self._running: request = await self._mailbox.dequeue()` (blocks until PlanRequest available). After dequeue: (1) check if `request.request_id in _cancel_set` -- if yes, remove from set, emit plan.cancelled.v1{request_id, reason: "cancelled_before_start"}, `continue` to next iteration (do NOT acquire lock). (2) `await self._plan_lock.acquire()`. (3) `try: cancel_check = lambda: request.request_id in self._cancel_set; committed = await self._pipeline.execute(request, cancel_check)`. (4) `finally: self._plan_lock.release(); self._pipeline.reset()` (PLAN_END: clear state, emit plan_end delta). **Error handling**: if `pipeline.execute()` raises PlannerError (FAILED), the error is already handled inside PipelineController (plan.failed.v1 emitted). PlannerAgent catches, logs, and continues loop. If unexpected exception: log error, emit plan.failed.v1{error_code: "INTERNAL_ERROR"}, continue loop. **FIFO order**: requests processed in enqueue order (§24.1.3). No priority weighting in V1. **Queue behavior**: depth-5 FIFO. When plan is in flight, new requests queue normally. At depth 5, `enqueue()` raises MailboxFullError -> PlannerAdapter returns PlanAck(REJECTED). |
| 2.3.3 | Plan lock (asyncio.Lock, V1 single plan) | DONE | k1/planner/planner_agent.py [F02] | **Lock lifecycle** (§24.1.1): `_plan_lock: asyncio.Lock`. **Acquired**: PLAN_START step 3 (after mailbox dequeue, after cancel pre-check). **Released**: PLAN_END step 2 (in `finally` block after pipeline.execute() completes or raises). **Semantics**: exclusive (only one plan pipeline runs at a time), non-reentrant (PlannerAgent never re-acquires during a plan), FIFO for waiters (asyncio.Lock is FIFO, but V1 has at most 1 waiter since micro_replan is the only code that might await the lock while a plan is in flight). **FSM state mapping** (§18.8): IDLE = lock FREE, PLAN_START = lock ACQUIRED, active states (SKETCHING/EXPANDING/VALIDATING/COMMITTING) = lock HELD, terminal states (COMPLETED/FAILED/CANCELLED) trigger lock RELEASE. **Micro-replan interaction** (§24.3.1): `micro_replan()` calls `await _plan_lock.acquire()` which BLOCKS until current plan completes. With 10s caller-side timeout and typical plan duration 12s p50, micro-replan will almost always timeout during active plan (by design -- Orchestrator falls back to original plan). **V2 evolution**: per-plan lock + global concurrency limiter replaces single lock. |
| 2.3.4 | Cancel set management | DONE | k1/planner/planner_agent.py [F02] | **Data structure**: `_cancel_set: Set[str]` -- stores request_ids that have been cancelled. **Population**: `on_cancel(request_id)` adds to set. Called by MailboxAdapter when Orchestrator invokes `IPlannerMailbox.send_cancel(request_id)` (§24.2.1). **Consumption**: checked at 5 cancel checkpoints (§24.2.2): (1) PlannerAgent dequeue loop pre-check (2.3.2), (2)-(5) between stages via `cancel_check` closure passed to PipelineController (2.2.4). **Cleanup**: `_cancel_set.discard(request_id)` at PLAN_END for the completed/failed/cancelled request. Also cleared of the request_id on pre-execution cancel (dequeue check). **Edge cases** (§24.2.4): cancel after COMPLETED (no-op, plan already delivered), cancel after FAILED (no-op, already terminal), cancel for unknown request_id (added to set, never matched, cleaned at PLAN_END or removed if stale), cancel arrives before enqueue (added to set, immediate cancel at dequeue pre-check), double cancel same request_id (idempotent, Set ignores duplicates). **Thread safety** (§24.5): V1 runs on single event loop, no concurrent mutation of _cancel_set. asyncio tasks yield at `await` points, but _cancel_set is only mutated by on_cancel() and dequeue loop, never concurrently in V1. |
| 2.3.5 | INIT lifecycle phase | DONE | k1/planner/planner_agent.py [F02] | **Sequence** (§23.1): runs once at PlannerAgent.start(). **Step 1: CREATE SERVICES** -- in V1, services are pre-created by PlannerFactory and injected via constructor. PlannerAgent.start() validates that pipeline and ports are wired. **Step 2: WIRE 7 PORTS** -- all port references injected at construction, not resolved lazily. Ports: PORT_MAILBOX (MailboxAdapter, bounded queue depth 5), PORT_LLM (TestLLMAdapter V1 / LLMGatewayAdapter V2), PORT_FABRIC_RET (FabricRetrievalAdapter), PORT_STATE_READ (SessionStateReadAdapter, lock-free), PORT_BRIDGE (BridgeAdapter), PORT_DELTA (DeltaBusAdapter, fire-and-forget), PORT_EVENT (EventBusAdapter, pub/sub). **Step 3: SUBSCRIBE EVENTS** -- `_subscriptions.append(await _event_port.subscribe("k1.planner.plan.request.v1", _on_plan_request))`, same for plan.cancel.v1, hil.clarification_response.v1, hil.approval_response.v1 (4 subscriptions per §23.1 step 3). **Step 4: SET STATE** -- FSM -> IDLE (via pipeline.reset()),_plan_lock released (default),_cancel_set empty,_running = True. **Post-condition**: PlannerAgent enters _run_loop(), awaiting the first PlanRequest. **Event handler wiring**: `_on_plan_request` enqueues to _mailbox. `_on_plan_cancel` calls on_cancel(). `_on_hil_clarification` and `_on_hil_approval` delegate to HILCoordinator (accessed via pipeline's service references). |
| 2.3.6 | SHUTDOWN lifecycle phase | DONE | k1/planner/planner_agent.py [F02] | **Sequence** (§23.5): triggered by PlannerAgent.stop(). **Step 1: STOP ACCEPTING** -- set `_running = False`, MailboxAdapter rejects new enqueue() with ShutdownError. PlannerAdapter (Orchestrator side) sees AdapterException(SHUTTING_DOWN). **Step 2: DRAIN MAILBOX** -- for each queued PlanRequest in `_mailbox`: emit `plan.cancelled.v1{request_id, reason: "shutdown"}`, remove from queue. Ensures Orchestrator's PendingPlanContext timeout reaper can clean up. **Step 3: CANCEL IN-FLIGHT** -- if a plan is currently executing (lock held): add current request_id to _cancel_set, PipelineController checks flag at next await boundary, triggers CANCELLED. If mid-LLM call: LLM call completes or times out, then cancel detected. **Grace period**: configurable `config.shutdown_grace_period_ms` (default 5000ms). If in-flight plan does not reach terminal state within grace period, force-cancel and proceed. **Step 4: FLUSH DELTAS** -- `_delta_port.flush()` (if adapter supports flush method), best-effort. **Step 5: PERSIST PARTIAL STATE** -- if plan was in-flight, `await _bridge_port.persist_plan(partial_plan)` with completed stages and partial results. Fire-and-forget, shutdown does not block on persist. V1 simplification: may skip partial persist. **Step 6: RELEASE LOCK** -- `_plan_lock.release()` if held (safety release). **Step 7: UNSUBSCRIBE** -- `for sub in _subscriptions: await _event_port.unsubscribe(sub)`. **Step 8: LOG** -- structured log `shutdown.complete{plans_drained, in_flight_cancelled, deltas_flushed}`. |
| 2.3.7 | CRASH_RECOVERY lifecycle phase | DONE | k1/planner/planner_agent.py [F02] | **Sequence** (§23.6): runs during INIT (start()), before entering _run_loop(). **Step 1: CHECK WAL** -- `result = await _bridge_port.recall(query="planner_in_flight", selectors=["partial_plan"])`. If empty: no crash recovery needed, proceed to dequeue loop. **Step 2: EVALUATE STALENESS** -- for each partial plan in WAL: read `last_completed_stage` and `plan_start_time`. If `plan_start_time + 120_000ms < now_ms()`, plan is stale (context has drifted) -> discard. **Step 3a: RESUME** (if not stale, V2 feature) -- if last_completed_stage == SKETCH: resume at EXPAND with saved SketchResult. If EXPAND: resume at VALIDATE. If VALIDATE: resume at COMMIT. FSM set to appropriate state, PipelineController runs remaining stages. Trace_id preserved from WAL. **Step 3b: DISCARD** (if stale or V1 simplification) -- emit `plan.failed.v1{request_id, reason: "crash_recovery_discard", stage: last_known_stage}`. Clear partial plan from WAL. FSM -> IDLE. **Step 4: EMIT DELTA** -- `DeltaPayload(agent_id="planner", delta_type="crash_recovery", section="pipeline", data={"action": "resumed" or "discarded", "request_id": ..., "last_stage": ..., "staleness_ms": ...})`. **Step 5: LOG** -- `crash_recovery.complete{action, request_id, last_stage, staleness_ms}`. **V1 simplification** (§23.6 note): V1 does NOT implement full crash recovery. Any in-flight plan is treated as lost. Orchestrator's stale-context reaper (45s timeout) emits DEGRADED and task is re-dispatched at MEDIUM tier. Full crash recovery (steps 2-3a) is V2 requiring reliable WAL reads from K0. V1 implementation: check WAL, always discard, set IDLE. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File location**: `k1/planner/planner_agent.py` [F02]. Single file contains PlannerAgent class.
> - **Dependency layer**: Layer 3 (§30.6) -- top of the dependency tree. Imports from Layer 2 (PipelineController), Layer 1 (IMailboxPort, IEventPort, IBridgePort), Layer 0 (PlanRequest, PlannerConfig, PlanState, all error types, event topic constants).
> - **Consumers**: PlannerFactory (M6) creates PlannerAgent with fully-wired dependencies. K1 runtime calls `agent.start()` at boot and `agent.stop()` at shutdown.
> - **Event subscriptions** (4 per §23.1): plan.request.v1 (enqueue to mailbox), plan.cancel.v1 (add to cancel set), hil.clarification_response.v1 (delegate to HILCoordinator), hil.approval_response.v1 (delegate to HILCoordinator). All subscriptions stored in `_subscriptions` for cleanup at SHUTDOWN.
> - **PlannerAgent is NOT PlannerFactory**: PlannerAgent receives pre-constructed services and ports. It does NOT instantiate SketchService, ExpandService, etc. That is PlannerFactory's job (M6, Epic 6.3). PlannerAgent's constructor takes the already-wired PipelineController.
> - **Testing**: ~25 tests covering: dequeue loop processes requests in FIFO order, cancel pre-check skips cancelled requests, lock prevents concurrent plans, micro_replan acquires lock, INIT subscribes to 4 events, SHUTDOWN drains queue and cancels in-flight, CRASH_RECOVERY V1 discards and sets IDLE.
> - **Anti-hallucination**: PlannerAgent does NOT perform LLM calls, tool discovery, or stage logic. It is purely a lifecycle coordinator and concurrency manager. All pipeline intelligence is in PipelineController and stage services.

> - [x] `async _run_loop()` dequeues FIFO from mailbox, pre-checks cancel set, acquires lock, delegates to `pipeline.execute()`, releases lock in finally

## Milestone 3: Stage Services

> **Goal**: Implement all four pipeline stages as independent services.

### Epic 3.1: SketchService

**Goal**: Implement Stage 1 of the pipeline -- transforms raw user intent into a SketchResult (rough plan with capability references) via 3 concurrent discovery tool calls, slot-based LLM prompt composition, optional HIL clarification, and structured error recovery. ~60 tests.

> **Reference**: planner.md §6 (Stage 1 SKETCH Deep Dive), §6.2 (Discovery Tool Calls), §6.3 (LLM Call -- Prompt Assembly, HubRequest, Budget), §6.4 (HIL Clarification), §6.5 (SketchResult Output), §6.6 (ERR_SKETCH_FAIL Recovery), §17.3 (SketchService -- ~60 tests, constructor, test categories).
>
> - **Constructor** (§17.3): `SketchService(llm_port: ILLMPort, tool_router: ToolCallRouter, hil_coord: HILCoordinator, pipeline_ctrl: PipelineController)`.
> - **Ports used**: ILLMPort (direct), IFabricRetrievalPort + IStateReadPort + IBridgePort (via ToolCallRouter).
> - **Invariants enforced**: PLAN-02 (read-only tools, slot-based prompt, output_schema = SketchPlanSchema), PLAN-11 (budget from StageContext).
> - **3 concurrent tool calls** (§6.2): discover_capabilities (IFabricRetrievalPort, <50ms), query_planning_context (IStateReadPort, <10ms), recall_for_planning (IBridgePort, <100ms). Dispatched via `asyncio.gather()`. ToolCallRouter accounting: tool_call_count += 3 (of 6 max per PLAN-05).
> - **Slot-based prompt** (§6.3.1): SYSTEM = ROLE_DEFINITION + OUTPUT_SCHEMA. USER = INTENT + CAPABILITY_CATALOG + SESSION_CONTEXT + LONG_TERM_MEMORY + CONSTRAINTS + HIL_ADDENDUM (conditional). Total input estimate: 800-1200 tokens, leaving 800-1200 for LLM response (max_tokens: 2048).
> - **Truncation priority** (§6.3.1): (1) LONG_TERM_MEMORY -> 3 facts, (2) CAPABILITY_CATALOG -> top-5, (3) SESSION_CONTEXT -> omit history_recent, (4) INTENT -> never truncated.
> - **Error recovery** (§6.6): tool failure -> retry once within budget, proceed degraded. LLM fail -> retry with simplified prompt (remove K0 memory, reduce capabilities to top-3). Second LLM fail -> PLAN FAILED (FSM -> FAILED).

ToolCallRouter/HILCoordinator defined as local `@runtime_checkable` Protocol stubs (ToolCallRouterLike, HILCoordinatorLike) following OrchestratorService sentinel pattern -- replaced with real imports when M4 lands. **Internal state**: `_llm_port`, `_tool_router`, `_hil_coord`. No mutable state -- SketchService is stateless between calls. All per-plan state flows through StageContext and method parameters. **Public interface**: `async def execute(self, request: PlanRequest, ctx: StageContext) -> SketchResult` -- full SKETCH stage pipeline (tools -> prompt -> LLM -> parse -> optional HIL -> result). `async def micro_execute(self, request: MicroReplanRequest, ctx: StageContext) -> SketchResult` -- abbreviated micro-SKETCH for micro-replan (no HIL, reduced budget, uses MicroReplanRequest.remaining_steps + completed_results + discoveries). **Dependency layer**: Layer 2 (§30.6) -- imports Layer 1 ports (ILLMPort), Layer 2 services (ToolCallRouter, HILCoordinator), Layer 0 types (PlanRequest, SketchResult, RoughStep, StageContext, HubRequest, HubResponse). **Gotcha**: SketchService does NOT hold a reference to PipelineController (token recording is done by PipelineController after receiving SketchResult, not by SketchService). **Tests**: ~45 tests in tests/test_planner_sketch_3_1_1.py (constructor valid, None rejection, **slots**, properties, execute/micro_execute signatures, statelessness, protocol checks, dependency layer, exports, instance isolation). |

Module-level `SKETCH_OUTPUT_SCHEMA` (JSON Schema dict) and `SKETCH_TOOL_DEFINITIONS` (tuple of 4 OpenAI function-calling tool dicts: discover_capabilities, query_session_context, recall_long_term_memory, find_prompts). `find_prompts` enables meta-agent prompt template discovery during SKETCH -- backed by Fabric `FindPromptsHandler` -> `RetrievalLike`. **System prompt**: `_assemble_system_prompt(self) -> str` -- concise planning role + embedded SKETCH_OUTPUT_SCHEMA. **Initial messages**: `_build_initial_messages(self, intent, constraints?, hil_addendum?) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]` -- system + user messages, optional constraint hints (safety_band, temporal). **Tool definitions**: `_get_tool_definitions(self)` -- regular method (not @staticmethod), returns SKETCH_TOOL_DEFINITIONS. |

Each round: send messages+tools to LLM, if response has tool_calls dispatch them via `_dispatch_tool_call`, append results, continue. Exits when LLM returns content without tool_calls. Cancellation check per round via `ctx.cancel_check`. **Tool dispatch**: `_dispatch_tool_call(self, tool_call, request, ctx) -> Any` -- routes by function name to ToolCallRouter methods (discover, read_context, recall_memory, find_prompts). Handles malformed JSON args (falls back to {}), unknown tools (returns error dict), router exceptions (returns error dict). |

Returns (SketchResult, needs_clarification, clarification_question). Raises SketchFailedError on invalid JSON or missing required fields. **HubRequest construction**: `_build_hub_request(self, messages, ctx, tools?) -> HubRequest` with capability="CHAT", payload={messages, temperature=0.7, tools?}, constraints from ctx.stage_budget or defaults (2048/8000). |

If user responds (non-None): re-run agentic loop with clarification as hil_addendum in initial messages. If user returns None (timeout/skip): accept initial result as-is. HILCoordinator implements HILCoordinatorLike protocol (round_count, reset, request_clarification). No separate LLM call for question generation -- clarification_question comes from LLM output schema. |
| 3.1.6 | Error recovery (ERR_SKETCH_FAIL) | DONE | k1/planner/stages/sketch_service.py [F19] | **Execute flow**: try first attempt (with tools + HIL) -> catch SketchFailedError -> retry with simplified=True (no tools, no HIL, forces direct LLM answer). If retry also fails, raise SketchFailedError. **Failure scenarios**: tool call errors returned as error dicts (LLM adapts), empty LLM response raises, round limit exhaustion raises, JSON parse failure raises, cancellation raises. **micro_execute**: builds intent from MicroReplanRequest (failure_context + remaining step capabilities), runs agentic loop, no HIL, no retry. |
| 3.1.7 | Module constants & no-static-method enforcement | DONE | k1/planner/stages/sketch_service.py [F19] | **Constants**: `_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS = 6`. Budget via `ctx.stage_budget` or defaults (max_tokens=2048, timeout_ms=8000). **No @staticmethod**: all methods are regular instance methods. **Tests**: ~92 tests in tests/test_planner_sketch_3_1_2.py across 15 test classes (schema, tool defs, system prompt, initial messages, tool definitions, hub request, dispatch, parse, agentic loop, execute, error recovery, HIL, micro_execute, no-static-methods, constants). |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File location**: `k1/planner/stages/sketch_service.py` [F19]. Single file contains SketchService class.
> - **Dependency layer**: Layer 2 (§30.6). Imports from Layer 1 (ILLMPort), Layer 2 (ToolCallRouter, HILCoordinator), Layer 0 (PlanRequest, MicroReplanRequest, SketchResult, RoughStep, StageContext, HubRequest, HubResponse, ScoredCapability, SketchFailedError, DeltaPayload).
> - **Creation -> Consumption**: SketchResult created by SketchService.execute() -> returned to PipelineController -> passed to ExpandService.execute() as input. capability_candidates (List[ScoredCapability]) carried forward for EXPAND refined discovery.
> - **Testing**: ~60 tests (§17.3): prompt slot composition (~15), LLM call correctness (~10), parse success (~10), parse failure + retry (~10), HIL clarification trigger (~10), error recovery ERR_SKETCH_FAIL (~5).
> - **Anti-hallucination**: SketchService does NOT write to SessionState (PLAN-01), does NOT execute capabilities (PLAN-06), does NOT emit deltas directly (PipelineController owns delta emission for stage_complete). SketchService's I/O is: (1) ToolCallRouter calls (read-only), (2) one ILLMPort.execute() call, (3) optional HILCoordinator.request_clarification(). No other side effects.

### Epic 3.2: ExpandService (Agentic Tool-Calling)

**Goal**: Implement Stage 2 of the pipeline -- transforms SketchResult into a fully parameterized ExpandedPlan (List[PlanStep] with 14 fields each) via agentic tool-calling loop (same mechanism as SKETCH), deterministic post-LLM enrichment of infrastructure fields, and fallback-capable error recovery. ~50 tests.

**SKETCH vs EXPAND**: Same agentic mechanism, different purpose. SKETCH answers "what should we do?" (creative, 0.7 temp). EXPAND answers "how exactly do we do it?" (precise, 0.3 temp). SKETCH produces rough intents. EXPAND produces fully parameterized executable steps. SKETCH failure kills the plan. EXPAND failure gracefully degrades. VALIDATE can send EXPAND back for revision without redoing SKETCH.

> **Reference**: planner.md §7 (Stage 2 EXPAND Deep Dive -- Agentic Architecture), §7.1 (Inputs -- SketchResult + PlanRequest), §7.2 (Agentic Tool-Calling Architecture -- 4 tools, tool routing, agentic loop), §7.3 (LLM Interaction -- system prompt, initial messages, HubRequest), §7.4 (ExpandedPlan Output -- EXPAND_OUTPUT_SCHEMA, post-LLM enrichment, field population matrix), §7.5 (Meta-Agent DAG Pattern), §7.6 (ERR_EXPAND_FAIL Recovery -- try/retry/fallback), §17.4 (ExpandService -- ~50 tests, constructor, test categories).
>
> - **Constructor** (§17.4): `ExpandService(llm_port: ILLMPort, tool_router: ToolCallRouter)`.
> - **Ports used**: ILLMPort (multiple rounds in agentic loop), ToolCallRouter (routes LLM-invoked tool calls to backends).
> - **No HIL**: EXPAND has NO HIL interaction point. Clarification happens in SKETCH, approval in VALIDATE (§7, paragraph 2).
> - **Agentic tool-calling** (§7.2): Same pattern as SKETCH -- LLM receives tool definitions and decides which to call across multiple rounds. 4 tools backed by Fabric handlers (`k1/fabric/core/discovery_tools.py`): discover_capabilities (`DiscoverCapabilitiesHandler`), get_capability_schema (`GetCapabilitySchemaHandler`), find_prompts (`FindPromptsHandler`), query_session_context (`IStateReadPort`). LLM decides what it needs -- simple intents may need zero tool calls.
> - **Post-LLM enrichment** (§7.4.2): 6 of 14 PlanStep fields are NOT LLM-generated -- they are deterministically populated from CapabilityContract metadata: has_side_effects, compensation, timeout_ms, required_context, safety_band_min, condition. This separation prevents hallucination of infrastructure fields.
> - **Meta-agent pattern** (§7.5): if plan requires dynamically created agent, EXPAND produces `build_agent` PlanStep with capability="tool.meta.build_agent" and AgentSpec in params. Planner NEVER executes agents (PLAN-06).

Same pattern as SKETCH (§6.2.3): iterate 1..MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS. Each round: send messages + EXPAND_TOOL_DEFINITIONS to ILLMPort.execute(). If response has tool_calls: dispatch each via `_dispatch_tool_call()`, append assistant + tool results to conversation, continue. If response has content (final answer): return (content, accumulated_results), exit loop. If MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS exhausted: raise ExpandFailedError. Check ctx.cancel_check each round. **Tool dispatch** (§7.2.2): `async def _dispatch_tool_call(self, tool_name, tool_args, ctx) -> Dict`. Routes to Fabric handlers via ToolCallRouter: discover_capabilities -> `ToolCallRouter.discover()` -> `DiscoverCapabilitiesHandler`, get_capability_schema -> `ToolCallRouter.get_schema()` -> `GetCapabilitySchemaHandler`, find_prompts -> `ToolCallRouter.find_prompts()` -> `FindPromptsHandler`, query_session_context -> `ToolCallRouter.read_context()` -> `IStateReadPort`. Unknown tool -> return error dict. Each tool call failure returns error dict (LLM adapts, loop continues). **HubRequest per round** (§7.3.3): capability=CHAT, temperature=0.3 (precision), max_tokens=ctx.stage_budget or 1024, timeout_ms=ctx.stage_budget or 5000, tools=EXPAND_TOOL_DEFINITIONS (when use_tools=True), trace_id=ctx.trace_id, consumer_id="planner.expand". **Key difference from SKETCH**: temperature 0.3 (not 0.7), no HIL interruption, SKETCH output in initial prompt provides strong context so LLM may need fewer tool rounds. |

Raise ExpandFailedError if invalid. **Post-LLM enrichment** (§7.4.2): `_enrich_steps(self, llm_steps: List[Dict], accumulated_results: Dict) -> List[PlanStep]`. For each step: lookup CapabilityContract from accumulated tool call results (discover_capabilities and get_capability_schema results) by capability name. Deterministically set: `has_side_effects` (infer from contract metadata), `compensation` (infer from contract), `timeout_ms` = contract.avg_latency_ms * 2 (or default 10000), `required_context` = contract.required_context[], `safety_band_min` = contract.safety_band_min, `condition` = None (V1). LLM-generated fields (8): id, capability, params, deps, prompt_template, tools_granted, output_schema, is_optional. If LLM omitted output_schema and contract.output exists, fill from contract. **ExpandedPlan construction**: `ExpandedPlan(steps=enriched_plan_steps, dependencies=parsed["dependencies"], tool_mappings={s.id: s.capability for s in steps}, rationale=parsed["rationale"])`. **Inter-step references**: params may contain `$<step_id>.result.<field>` syntax -- symbolic, resolved by Orchestrator at execution time. VALIDATE checks that referenced step_ids appear in deps. **Meta-agent** (§7.5): if step uses capability="tool.meta.build_agent", params contain AgentSpec (name, role, tools_granted, prompt_template, seed_context, ttl_turns). has_side_effects=true for build_agent. |
| 3.2.5 | Error recovery & fallback (ERR_EXPAND_FAIL) | DONE | k1/planner/stages/expand_service.py [F20] | **Execute flow** (§7.6.1): `execute()` wraps `_execute_attempt()` in try/retry/fallback structure. Same pattern as SKETCH but with FALLBACK instead of PLAN FAILED. **1st attempt**: `_execute_attempt(sketch_result, request, ctx, arbiter_feedback)` -- builds messages, runs agentic loop with tools, parses, enriches. **On ExpandFailedError**: retry with `_execute_attempt(sketch_result, request, ctx, simplified=True)` -- simplified mode: no tools, forces direct LLM parameterization from prompt context alone. **On 2nd failure**: FALLBACK via `_build_degraded_plan(sketch_result)` -- converts RoughSteps to minimal PlanSteps: `PlanStep(id="s<n>", capability=step.suggested_capability or "UNRESOLVED", params={}, timeout_ms=10000)`. These proceed to VALIDATE (pipeline continues rather than dying). **Failure scenarios** (§7.6.2): tool call failure -> _dispatch returns error dict, LLM adapts; LLM empty response -> raises ExpandFailedError; round limit exhausted -> raises after MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS; JSON parse failure -> raises ExpandFailedError; missing steps -> raises; invalid capability -> enrichment uses fallback defaults; invalid deps (cycle) -> remove circular edges, flatten to sequential; cancellation -> raises ExpandFailedError. **Key difference from SKETCH**: EXPAND has FALLBACK (degraded plan from SKETCH output), not PLAN FAILED. Only SKETCH failure terminates the entire plan. **Stage delta on fallback**: PipelineController emits `{stage: "EXPAND", status: "degraded", degradation_reason: "expand_llm_fallback"}`. |

ExpandService injects feedback into initial messages via `_build_initial_messages()`: user message includes "ARBITER FEEDBACK: The following issues were found..." + verdict.reasons[] + suggested_fixes[]. This gives the LLM targeted guidance for the re-run. Max 1 revise loop, max 2 total EXPAND runs. **Token budget** (§7.3.3): PipelineController injects EXPAND budget via StageContext. ExpandService reads ctx.token_budget (or defaults to 1024). HubRequest constraints: max_tokens=1024, timeout_ms=5000, temperature=0.3. Enforced by Model Hub (MH-04 truncates, LLMGatewayAdapter cancels on timeout). Token counting: tokens_used accumulated across all agentic loop rounds. Returned for PipelineController to accumulate in stage_token_usage[EXPAND]. **Revise loop token impact** (§8.5.3): each revise adds ~1K tokens (EXPAND re-run) + ~500 tokens (VALIDATE re-run) = ~1.5K per loop. Worst case: 3.5K (normal) + 3K (2 loops) = ~6.5K total plan tokens. **Micro-replan budget** (§10.3.6): MICRO_EXPAND uses {max_tokens: 512, timeout_ms: 3000} -- reduced budget via StageContext. **Module constants enforcement**: all constants module-level, no static methods, no class-level state, all methods instance methods on `self`. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File location**: `k1/planner/stages/expand_service.py` [F20]. Single file contains ExpandService class.
> - **Dependency layer**: Layer 2 (§30.6). Imports from Layer 1 (ILLMPort), Layer 2 (ToolCallRouter), Layer 0 (SketchResult, ExpandedPlan, PlanStep, PlanRequest, MicroReplanRequest, StageContext, HubRequest, HubResponse, ScoredCapability, RoughStep, ExpandFailedError).
> - **Pattern reference**: sketch_service.py -- ExpandService mirrors the agentic pattern established in SKETCH (agentic loop, tool dispatch, response parsing, try/retry structure). Same ToolCallRouterLike Protocol (extended with `get_schema(capability_name, version?)` -> `RegistryLookupLike` and `find_prompts(intent, domain?, top_k?)` -> `RetrievalLike` methods). Fabric backend handlers in `k1/fabric/core/discovery_tools.py`: `DiscoverCapabilitiesHandler`, `GetCapabilitySchemaHandler`, `FindPromptsHandler`.
> - **Creation -> Consumption**: ExpandedPlan created by ExpandService.execute() -> returned to PipelineController -> passed to ValidateService.execute() AND CommitService.execute() (if approved). ExpandedPlan.steps (List[PlanStep]) are frozen and carried forward unchanged to CommittedPlan.
> - **Revise loop interaction** (§8.5): if ValidateService returns verdict="revise", PipelineController calls `expand.execute(sketch_result, request, ctx, arbiter_feedback=verdict.suggested_fixes)`. ExpandService injects arbiter feedback into initial messages. Max 1 revise loop, max 2 total EXPAND runs.
> - **Testing**: ~50 tests (§17.4): output schema & tool definitions (~8), agentic loop & tool dispatch (~12), response parsing & enrichment (~10), error recovery & fallback (~10), arbiter feedback injection (~5), edge cases (~5).
> - **Anti-hallucination**: ExpandService does NOT write to SessionState (PLAN-01), does NOT execute capabilities (PLAN-06), does NOT interact with HILCoordinator. ExpandService's I/O is: (1) ToolCallRouter calls (read-only, LLM-initiated via agentic loop), (2) ILLMPort.execute() calls (multiple rounds possible). No other side effects. Infrastructure fields on PlanStep (has_side_effects, timeout_ms, etc.) are deterministic from CapabilityContract, NOT from LLM output.

### Epic 3.3: ValidateService

**Goal**: Implement Stage 3 of the pipeline -- two-phase validation (deterministic structural checks + LLM arbiter semantic review), optional HIL approval for high-impact plans, and ValidationVerdict assembly that routes to COMMIT (approved), EXPAND (revise), or FAILED (reject). ~45 tests.

> **Reference**: planner.md §8 (Stage 3 VALIDATE Deep Dive), §8.1 (Inputs -- ExpandedPlan + PlanRequest), §8.2 (Deterministic Checks -- DAG cycle + capability existence), §8.3 (LLM Arbiter -- prompt assembly, HubRequest, ValidationVerdict type), §8.4 (HIL Approval -- trigger conditions, flow, response routing), §8.5 (Revise Loop -- protocol, bounds, token impact), §8.6 (ERR_VALIDATE_FAIL Recovery), §17.5 (ValidateService -- ~45 tests, constructor, test categories).
>
> - **Constructor** (§17.5): `ValidateService(llm_port: ILLMPort, fabric_retrieval: IFabricRetrievalPort, hil_coord: HILCoordinator)`.
> - **Ports used**: ILLMPort (direct -- arbiter call), IFabricRetrievalPort (direct -- capability existence check, NOT via ToolCallRouter).
> - **ZERO tool calls** through ToolCallRouter. Capability existence uses IFabricRetrievalPort directly or cached discovery results from EXPAND.
> - **Invariants enforced**: PLAN-02 (slot-based prompt), PLAN-06/PLAN-09 (DAG acyclicity via Kahn's algorithm), PLAN-08 (capability existence in Fabric Registry), PLAN-05 (max tool call count validation -- read from ToolCallRouter counter).
> - **Two-phase validation**: Phase 1 = deterministic checks (no LLM, <5ms). Phase 2 = LLM arbiter (STRUCTURED capability, ~512 tokens, temperature=0.1). If Phase 1 fails, Phase 2 skipped (save tokens).
> - **Revise loop** (§8.5): verdict="revise" -> PipelineController re-runs EXPAND with suggested_fixes, then re-runs VALIDATE. Max 1 revise loop, max 2 EXPAND re-runs total. Second "revise" treated as "approved" (best-effort).
> - **HIL approval** (§8.4): triggered AFTER arbiter approves, when plan has high-impact side effects AND non-GREEN safety band. Auto-approve if all steps safe (GREEN + assessment="safe"). Timeout: 120s.

This does NOT count against PLAN-05 tool call budget (ToolCallRouter is not involved). |
| 3.3.2 | DAG cycle detection (Kahn's topological sort, PLAN-09) | TODO | k1/planner/stages/validate_service.py [F21] | **Algorithm** (§8.2.1): Kahn's topological sort. **Input**: `ExpandedPlan.dependencies: Dict[str, List[str]]` + `ExpandedPlan.steps[].id`. **Steps**: (1) Build adjacency list from dependencies dict (dep_id -> step_id edges). (2) Compute in-degree per step. (3) Initialize queue with zero-in-degree steps. (4) While queue non-empty: dequeue, decrement successors' in-degree, enqueue new zero-in-degree nodes. (5) If visited == len(steps): PASS (acyclic). If visited < len(steps): FAIL (cycle detected). **Additional structural checks** (§8.2.1 table): self-reference (no step depends on itself), dangling reference (all dep IDs reference existing steps), inter-step ref consistency (`$step_id.result.*` params must have matching deps entry), step ID uniqueness (all step.id values distinct). **Complexity**: O(V + E), sub-millisecond for typical plans (4-10 steps). **Private method**: `_check_dag_acyclicity(self, steps: List[PlanStep], dependencies: Dict[str, List[str]]) -> DagCheckResult` where `DagCheckResult = NamedTuple(passed: bool, cycle_path: Optional[List[str]], structural_errors: List[str])`. **On failure**: return rejection verdict immediately without calling LLM arbiter: `ValidationVerdict(status="reject", reasons=["DAG cycle detected: s2 -> s3 -> s2"], deterministic_pass=False)`. |
| 3.3.3 | Capability existence check (PLAN-08) | TODO | k1/planner/stages/validate_service.py [F21] | **Purpose** (§8.2.2): verify every PlanStep.capability references a real capability in Fabric Registry. Prevents LLM hallucination of capability names. **Input**: `ExpandedPlan.steps[].capability`. **Procedure**: for each step: (1) if capability starts with "$" -> SKIP (inter-step reference, resolved at execution time). (2) if capability == "tool.meta.build_agent" -> SKIP (reserved meta-capability, always valid). (3) else -> query IFabricRetrievalPort or check cached EXPAND discovery results. If found: PASS. If not found: FAIL with unknown capability. **Route**: ValidateService -> IFabricRetrievalPort directly (preferred: check cached RetrievalResult from EXPAND stage to avoid redundant I/O). **Private method**: `_check_capability_existence(self, steps: List[PlanStep], cached_capabilities: Optional[List[ScoredCapability]]) -> CapCheckResult` where `CapCheckResult = NamedTuple(passed: bool, missing: List[str])`. **On failure**: rejection verdict: `ValidationVerdict(status="reject", reasons=["Unknown capability: tool.execute.nonexistent (step s2)"], suggested_fixes=["Replace with tool.execute.similar from registry"], deterministic_pass=False)`. **Combined check** (§8.2.3): DAG check runs first, then capability check. Both sequential, total <5ms. If EITHER fails: deterministic_pass=False, do NOT call LLM arbiter (save tokens). |

If PlanStep has required param missing -> add ValidationIssue with severity="error". If PlanStep has extra params not in contract.optional_inputs -> add ValidationIssue with severity="warning". **Private method**: `_validate_plan_constraints(self, steps: List[PlanStep], ctx: StageContext, cached_capabilities: Optional[List[ScoredCapability]]) -> List[ValidationIssue]`. Capabilities: all N verified."), CONSTRAINTS (safety_band, user limits). **Token budget**: system ~150, user 200-350. Temperature 0.1 -- lowest of all stages, deterministic verdicts preferred. **Output schema** (§8.3.1): status (enum: "approved"/"revise"/"reject"), reasons (string array, minItems 1), suggested_fixes (string array), coherence_score (0.0-1.0), safety_assessment (enum: "safe"/"caution"/"unsafe"), completeness (boolean). **5 arbiter dimensions** (§8.3.1): Coherence (steps serve intent?), Safety (side effects appropriate for safety_band?), Completeness (all intent aspects covered?), Feasibility (params reasonable, deps satisfiable?), Redundancy (duplicate/unnecessary steps?). **Private methods**: `_assemble_arbiter_prompt(self, expanded_plan, intent, constraints, det_results) -> (str, str)`, `_parse_verdict(self, content: str, deterministic_pass: bool) -> ValidationVerdict`. |
| 3.3.6 | Optional HIL approval flow | TODO | k1/planner/stages/validate_service.py [F21] | **Trigger conditions** (§8.4.1): ALL must be true: (1) high-impact operations -- at least one step has has_side_effects=true AND side effect is external (booking, ordering, sending), (2) not auto-approvable -- plan has step with safety_band_min higher than GREEN OR arbiter safety_assessment="caution", (3) LLM arbiter approved -- arbiter returned status="approved" (if "revise"/"reject", no HIL). **Auto-approve rule**: if ALL side-effect steps have safety_band_min="GREEN" AND arbiter safety_assessment="safe", auto-approve without user interaction (common case). **Flow** (§8.4.2): `await _hil_coord.request_approval(request_id, plan_summary)` -> HILCoordinator generates approval presentation via ILLMPort (HubRequest CHAT, max_tokens=400, timeout_ms=3000), emits `k1.hil.approval_request.v1{request_id, summary, options: ["approve","modify","reject"], side_effects, safety_assessment, estimated_duration_ms}`. Route: IEventPort -> Concierge -> User. Wait for `k1.hil.approval_response.v1`, timeout 120s. **Response routing** (§8.4.3): "approve" -> proceed to COMMIT. "modify" -> apply field-level overrides per step, re-run deterministic checks (DAG may change), proceed to COMMIT (no additional arbiter call). "reject" -> PLAN FAILED. Timeout with all GREEN steps -> auto-approve. Timeout with non-GREEN -> PLAN FAILED. **Private method**: `_check_and_run_hil_approval(self, expanded_plan, verdict, request, ctx) -> ValidationVerdict` -- may upgrade verdict to include HIL decision. |
| 3.3.7 | ValidationVerdict assembly | TODO | k1/planner/stages/validate_service.py [F21] | **ValidationVerdict type** (§8.3.3): `ValidationVerdict(status: str, reasons: List[str], suggested_fixes: List[str], coherence_score: float, safety_assessment: str, completeness: bool, deterministic_pass: bool)`. **Assembly flow**: (1) Run deterministic checks (3.3.2 DAG + 3.3.3 capability). If fails -> immediate rejection verdict with deterministic_pass=False. (2) Run constraint validation (3.3.4). (3) If deterministic_pass=True -> run LLM arbiter (3.3.5). Parse structured response into typed ValidationVerdict fields. Set deterministic_pass=True. (4) If arbiter approved -> check HIL trigger (3.3.6). If HIL triggered and user rejects -> override status to "reject". If user modifies -> apply mods, re-run deterministic checks, status stays "approved". (5) Return final ValidationVerdict. **Consumed by**: PipelineController routes based on status: "approved" -> COMMIT, "revise" -> re-run EXPAND with suggested_fixes (max 1 loop), "reject" -> PLAN FAILED. Second "revise" treated as "approved" (best-effort per §8.5.2). Second "reject" -> terminal FAILED. **Stage delta** (§8.6.3): `{type: "stage_complete", stage: "VALIDATE", status: "completed"/"degraded"/"failed", tokens_used, tool_calls_used: 0, hil_rounds: 0/1, duration_ms, verdict: "approved"/"revise"/"reject"/"auto_approved", revise_loops: 0/1/2, deterministic_pass}`. The "auto_approved" verdict signals arbiter unavailable but deterministic checks passed. |
| 3.3.8 | Error recovery (ERR_VALIDATE_FAIL) | TODO | k1/planner/stages/validate_service.py [F21] | **Recovery paths** (§8.6): **Path 1 -- Deterministic check failure** (DAG cycle or unknown capability): route verdict.suggested_fixes to EXPAND for re-run via revise loop (§8.5). If re-run also fails deterministic checks: PLAN FAILED (unusual -- indicates EXPAND LLM produced structurally invalid output). **Path 2 -- LLM arbiter timeout/error** (deterministic checks PASSED): AUTO-APPROVE. Rationale: structural integrity confirmed, LLM quality check is nice-to-have. Log warning "arbiter_unavailable, auto_approved_on_deterministic_pass". Return `ValidationVerdict(status="approved", deterministic_pass=True, safety_assessment="unknown")`. This is the key graceful degradation: plans proceed without LLM oversight when arbiter down, as long as structural checks pass. **Path 3 -- LLM arbiter timeout/error** (deterministic checks FAILED): PLAN FAILED (cannot auto-approve structurally invalid plan). **Path 4 -- Verdict "reject" after revise loop exhausted**: PLAN FAILED. FSM -> FAILED. Emit `plan.failed.v1{stage: "VALIDATE", error: "ERR_VALIDATE_FAIL", message: "Plan rejected by arbiter after retry", reasons: verdict.reasons}`. **Path 5 -- HIL approval timeout**: auto-approve if all steps safe (all safety_band_min=="GREEN"), otherwise PLAN FAILED. **Path 6 -- HIL approval explicit reject**: PLAN FAILED (explicit user rejection is final, no retry). **Token budget enforcement**: max_tokens=512, timeout_ms=3000. Micro-VALIDATE: {max_tokens: 256, timeout_ms: 2000}. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File location**: `k1/planner/stages/validate_service.py` [F21]. Single file contains ValidateService class.
> - **Dependency layer**: Layer 2 (§30.6). Imports from Layer 1 (ILLMPort, IFabricRetrievalPort), Layer 2 (HILCoordinator), Layer 0 (ExpandedPlan, PlanRequest, ValidationVerdict, ValidationIssue, PlanStep, StageContext, HubRequest, HubResponse, ScoredCapability, ValidateRejectedError).
> - **Creation -> Consumption**: ValidationVerdict created by ValidateService.execute() -> returned to PipelineController -> consumed for routing (COMMIT/EXPAND revise/FAILED). Also passed to CommitService.execute() for audit trail recording in stage delta.
> - **Revise loop** (§8.5): PipelineController manages the revise loop -- if verdict="revise" and revise_count < 1, PipelineController re-runs ExpandService then ValidateService. ValidateService does NOT self-loop. Timing impact: normal 9s, 1 revise 14s, max 2 loops 19s -- all within 45s budget.
> - **Testing**: ~45 tests (§17.5): DAG cycle detection (~8), capability existence (~8), schema compatibility (~5), safety band check (~5), LLM arbiter call (~8), HIL approval flow (~6), error recovery ERR_VALIDATE_FAIL (~5).
> - **Anti-hallucination**: ValidateService does NOT write to SessionState (PLAN-01), does NOT execute capabilities (PLAN-06), does NOT use ToolCallRouter (zero tool calls counted against PLAN-05). ValidateService's I/O is: (1) IFabricRetrievalPort direct call (capability check, NOT via ToolCallRouter), (2) one ILLMPort.execute() call (STRUCTURED capability), (3) optional HILCoordinator.request_approval(). No other side effects.

### Epic 3.4: CommitService

**Goal**: Implement Stage 4 of the pipeline -- purely deterministic CommittedPlan assembly (zero LLM calls enforced at constructor level via PLAN-03), WAL persistence (fire-and-forget via IBridgePort), plan delivery event emission (plan.ready.v1), and stage/plan-end delta emissions. ~30 tests. Performance: 0 tokens, p50 20ms / p99 100ms.

> **Reference**: planner.md §9 (Stage 4 COMMIT Deep Dive), §9.1 (Inputs -- ExpandedPlan + PlanRequest + ValidationVerdict), §9.2 (CommittedPlan Assembly -- 11 steps, 7 defensive validations), §9.3 (K0 WAL Persistence -- fire-and-forget, idempotent), §9.4 (Plan Delivery -- event emission, correlation contract), §9.5 (Plan FSM Transition -- COMMITTING -> COMPLETED -> IDLE), §9.6 (ERR_COMMIT_FAIL Recovery), §17.6 (CommitService -- ~30 tests, constructor, test categories).
>
> - **Constructor** (§17.6): `CommitService(bridge_port: IBridgePort, delta_port: IDeltaEmitPort, event_port: IEventPort)`.
> - **PLAN-03 enforcement**: constructor has NO `ILLMPort` parameter. Zero LLM calls by design, enforced at interface level (not just convention). Zero tokens consumed in COMMIT stage.
> - **Ports used**: IBridgePort (WAL persist), IDeltaEmitPort (stage + plan_end deltas), IEventPort (plan.ready.v1 delivery).
> - **No dependencies on**: ILLMPort (PLAN-03), IFabricRetrievalPort (no discovery), IStateReadPort (no session reads), ToolCallRouter (no tool calls), HILCoordinator (no HIL).
> - **CommittedPlan** (§9.2.2): frozen dataclass from `k1/orchestrator/types.py` with plan_id, request_id, intent, steps (List[PlanStep]), trace_id, dependencies, estimated_duration_ms, created_at. Serialized via `to_dict()` for event payload. Deserialized by Orchestrator via `CommittedPlan.from_dict()`.
> - **Correlation contract**: `CommittedPlan.request_id` MUST match original `PlanRequest.request_id` (Orchestrator matches to PendingPlanContext).

On validation failure: raise ValueError (should never happen if VALIDATE passed). **Private methods**: `_build_committed_plan(self, expanded_plan, request) -> CommittedPlan`, `_compute_critical_path_duration(self, steps, dependencies) -> int`. **Meta-agent** (§9.2.3): if plan includes build_agent steps, CommittedPlan contains PlanSteps with capability="tool.meta.build_agent" and `$s2.result.agent_name` symbolic references -- resolved by Orchestrator at execution time (PLAN-06). |
| 3.4.4 | WAL persist via IBridgePort | TODO | k1/planner/stages/commit_service.py [F23] | **Persistence protocol** (§9.3): `await _bridge_port.persist_plan(committed_plan)`. **Properties**: fire-and-forget (CommitService does NOT await confirmation beyond the async call return), idempotent (plan_id is dedup key, re-persisting is a no-op via WAL upsert semantics), best-effort (plan validity does NOT depend on WAL success -- plan is correct and deliverable even if WAL fails). **Route**: BridgeAdapter -> K0 Bridge -> IKernelCommandPort -> WAL write. **On success**: log `"commit.wal_persisted" {plan_id, duration_ms}`. **On failure**: log warning `"commit.wal_persist_failed" {plan_id, error}`. Retry once (same call, same plan_id -- idempotent). If retry also fails: log error, proceed to plan delivery anyway. WAL failure NEVER blocks plan delivery. **Why fire-and-forget** (§9.3.2): K0 WAL serves durability (crash recovery), not correctness. CommittedPlan is self-contained. If WAL fails, plan still reaches Orchestrator via event bus. On crash recovery, Orchestrator re-requests via PendingPlanContext timeout. WAL is optimization for faster recovery, not prerequisite. **Private method**: `async def _persist_to_wal(self, committed_plan: CommittedPlan) -> bool` -- returns True if persisted, False on failure (caller proceeds either way). |

Route: EventBusAdapter -> K1 Event Bus -> Orchestrator subscription (`_on_plan_ready()` handler). **Consumers**: Orchestrator (primary) -- deserializes via `CommittedPlan.from_dict(payload)`, enqueues to Orchestrator Mailbox at INTERACTIVE priority, correlates via request_id -> PendingPlanContext. Learning Loop (observability) -- records plan metadata for drift detection. **Correlation contract** (§9.4.3): `committed_plan.request_id` MUST match `PlanRequest.request_id`. If no matching PendingPlanContext (orphaned plan from timeout reaper), Orchestrator logs warning and drops. **Failure events** (§9.4.4): if plan failed at ANY stage, PipelineController (not CommitService) emits `k1.planner.plan.failed.v1{request_id, reason, stage, error_details, trace_id}` instead. If cancelled: `k1.planner.plan.cancelled.v1{request_id, reason, trace_id}`. **Micro-replan delivery** (§10.1.2): micro_replan.ready.v1 is TELEMETRY-ONLY (emitted for Learning Loop, NOT for delivery). Delivery is via synchronous return value from `IPlannerMailbox.micro_replan()`. **Private method**: `async def _deliver_plan(self, committed_plan: CommittedPlan) -> None` -- publish event, retry once on failure. If retry fails: plan completed but undeliverable (Orchestrator PendingPlanContext times out at 45s). |
| 3.4.6 | Delta emission (plan_committed + plan_end) | TODO | k1/planner/stages/commit_service.py [F23] | **Stage delta** (§9.5): on successful delivery, emit stage_complete delta: `DeltaPayload(agent_id="planner", delta_type="stage_complete", section="pipeline", data={"stage": "COMMIT", "status": "completed", "plan_id": plan_id, "tokens_used": 0, "tool_calls_used": 0, "duration_ms": commit_wall_time, "wal_persisted": True/False})`. **Plan-end delta** (§9.5): separate emission summarizing entire plan: `DeltaPayload(agent_id="planner", delta_type="plan_end", section="pipeline", data={"plan_id": plan_id, "total_tokens": sum(stage_token_usage), "total_duration_ms": plan_wall_time, "total_tool_calls": tool_call_count, "total_hil_rounds": hil_round_count, "revise_loops": revise_count, "stages_completed": ["SKETCH","EXPAND","VALIDATE","COMMIT"]})`. **Emission method**: `_delta_port.emit(delta)` -- synchronous, fire-and-forget, NEVER raises, NEVER blocks (§15.7). If Delta Bus unavailable, adapter logs warning and returns silently. **FSM transitions** (§9.5): COMMITTING -> COMPLETED (plan delivered). Then PipelineController triggers COMPLETED -> IDLE (ready for next request). Plan lock released. Counters and buffers cleared. **Complete COMMIT sequence** (§9.6.4, nominal path): [1] plan_id = uuid4(), [2] created_at = time.time(), [3] build CommittedPlan, [4] **post_init** validates, [5] persist WAL (fire-and-forget), [6] publish plan.ready.v1, [7] FSM COMMITTING->COMPLETED, [8] emit stage_complete delta, [9] emit plan_end delta, [10] release lock + clear state, [11] FSM COMPLETED->IDLE. Total: 11 steps, 0 LLM calls, 0 tokens, <100ms. |

>
> - **File location**: `k1/planner/stages/commit_service.py` [F23]. Single file contains CommitService class.
> - **Dependency layer**: Layer 2 (§30.6). Imports from Layer 1 (IBridgePort, IDeltaEmitPort, IEventPort), Layer 0 (ExpandedPlan, PlanRequest, CommittedPlan, PlanStep, ValidationVerdict, StageContext, DeltaPayload, CommitFailedError, TOPIC_PLAN_READY from events.py).
> - **Explicitly does NOT import**: ILLMPort, IFabricRetrievalPort, IStateReadPort, ToolCallRouter, HILCoordinator (PLAN-03 enforcement).
> - **Creation -> Consumption**: CommittedPlan created by CommitService.execute() -> event payload published to `k1.planner.plan.ready.v1` -> Orchestrator deserializes via `CommittedPlan.from_dict()`. Also persisted to K0 WAL via IBridgePort (fire-and-forget).
> - **Error recovery** (§9.6): WAL persist failure -> retry once, proceed anyway. Event publish failure -> retry once, on second failure plan is completed but undeliverable. CommittedPlan validation error -> PLAN FAILED (indicates VALIDATE bug). WAL failure NEVER blocks plan delivery.
> - **Testing**: ~30 tests (§17.6): plan assembly correctness (~8), WAL persist call (~6), event emission (~6), delta emission (~4), Bridge offline (~3), cancel during commit (~3).
> - **Anti-hallucination**: CommitService is the simplest stage -- purely deterministic, no LLM, no discovery, no HIL. Its only I/O is: (1) IBridgePort.persist_plan() fire-and-forget, (2) IEventPort.publish() plan delivery, (3) IDeltaEmitPort.emit() stage/plan-end deltas. Total execution under 100ms. Do NOT add any LLM, discovery, or tool call logic to this service.

## Milestone 4: Support Services

> **Goal**: Implement ToolCallRouter and HILCoordinator that serve the stage services.

### Epic 4.1: ToolCallRouter

**Goal**: Implement the deterministic ToolCallRouter service that maps 4 abstract discovery tool names to 3 backend ports (IFabricRetrievalPort, IStateReadPort, IBridgePort), enforces PLAN-05 (max 6 calls per plan) via monotonic counter, enforces PLAN-02 (read-only, zero side effects) and PLAN-06 (no execute route), handles per-tool timeouts and retries, and supports concurrent dispatch via asyncio.gather. ~35 tests.

> **Reference**: planner.md §11 (Discovery Tools Deep Dive), §11.1 (discover_capabilities -- 4-step retrieval pipeline, RetrievalResult, per-stage extraction), §11.2 (find_relevant_prompts -- prompt-type pre-filter, EXPAND-only caller), §11.3 (query_planning_context -- SessionState read, freshness protocol, PLAN-01), §11.4 (recall_for_planning -- K0 Bridge, health awareness, offline graceful degradation), §11.5 (ToolCallRouter Internal Design -- routing table, budget enforcement algorithm, retry/timeout policy, concurrency model), §17.7 (ToolCallRouter -- ~35 tests, constructor, test categories).
>
> - **Constructor** (§17.7): `ToolCallRouter(fabric_retrieval: IFabricRetrievalPort, state_read: IStateReadPort, bridge_port: IBridgePort)`.
> - **Ports used**: IFabricRetrievalPort (discover_capabilities + find_relevant_prompts), IStateReadPort (query_planning_context), IBridgePort (recall_for_planning). All direct.
> - **Routing table** (§11.5.1): 4 read-only tools -> 3 backend ports. Frozen class-level constant -- no dynamic registration.
> - **Invariants enforced**: PLAN-02 (all tools read-only, zero side effects), PLAN-05 (max 6 calls/plan, monotonic counter), PLAN-06 (no execute route in routing table).
> - **Counter lifecycle** (§11.5.2): `tool_call_count` monotonic counter, incremented on dispatch, reset at LC_PLAN_START and micro-replan start. Per-plan, NOT per-stage. Counter is the ONLY mutable instance state.
> - **Retry policy** (§11.5.3): discover_capabilities 50ms/1 retry, find_relevant_prompts 50ms/1 retry, query_planning_context 10ms/0 retries, recall_for_planning 100ms/0 retries. Retries do NOT double-count against PLAN-05.
> - **Concurrency** (§11.5.4): safe for concurrent dispatch (`asyncio.gather`). Counter increment is atomic-safe (single-threaded event loop, no interleaving within coroutine boundary per §24.1).

| 4.1.1 | ToolCallRouter skeleton & constructor | TODO | k1/planner/services/tool_call_router.py [F25] | **Signature**: `class ToolCallRouter` with constructor `__init__(self, fabric_retrieval: IFabricRetrievalPort, state_read: IStateReadPort, bridge_port: IBridgePort)`. **Internal state**: `_fabric_retrieval`, `_state_read`, `_bridge_port`, `_tool_call_count: int = 0`. Counter is the ONLY mutable instance state (unlike stage services which are fully stateless). **Routing table** (§11.5.1): class-level constant `_ROUTING_TABLE: Dict[str, str]` mapping tool name -> port attribute: `{"discover_capabilities": "_fabric_retrieval", "find_relevant_prompts": "_fabric_retrieval", "query_planning_context": "_state_read", "recall_for_planning": "_bridge_port"}`. Frozen -- no dynamic registration, no `register_tool()` or `add_route()` methods. **Public interface**: `async def call(self, tool_name: str, **params) -> Any` -- main dispatch (validate tool name, check budget, dispatch to port, increment counter, return result). `def reset(self) -> None` -- reset `tool_call_count` to 0 (called by PipelineController at LC_PLAN_START and micro-replan start). `@property def tool_call_count(self) -> int` -- current counter value (read by PipelineController for stage deltas and PLAN-05 post-hoc validation). **Convenience methods**: `async def discover(self, intent, domain=None, safety_band="GREEN", top_k=10) -> RetrievalResult`, `async def find_prompts(self, intent, domain=None, safety_band="GREEN", top_k=10) -> RetrievalResult`, `async def read_context(self, session_id, sections) -> Dict[str, Any]`, `async def recall_memory(self, query, trace_id) -> BridgeCommandResult`. Each delegates to `call()` internally. **Dependency layer**: Layer 2 (§30.6) -- imports Layer 1 (IFabricRetrievalPort, IStateReadPort, IBridgePort), Layer 0 (RetrievalResult, BridgeCommandResult, SessionSnapshot, ToolCallStatus, BudgetExhaustedError, UnknownToolError, PlannerConfig). |

Runs 4-step retrieval pipeline (§11.1.2): Step 0 GATHER (domain tag filter or full scan), Step 1 EMBED (intent -> 384-dim vector via ultrabert-v4.0.0), Step 2 HARD FILTER (safety band / availability / input satisfiability, 3 elimination rules), Step 3 SOFT RANK (4-dimension weighted composite: 0.40*semantic + 0.30*domain + 0.15*success_rate + 0.15*cost_latency, DEGRADED penalty *= 0.70), Step 4 TOP-K SELECT (clamp to [1, max_k=25]). **Return type** (§11.1.3): `RetrievalResult(capabilities: List[ScoredCapability], total_matched, query_latency_ms, query_intent, index_size, embedding_model)`. Each ScoredCapability wraps full CapabilityContract (~30 fields) + composite score [0.0, 1.0]. **SKETCH usage** (§11.1.4): broad intent, top_k=10, builds "capability menu" for LLM prompt CAPABILITY_CATALOG slot. **EXPAND usage** (§11.1.4): narrow per-step intent, top_k=3-5, resolves full CapabilityContract for PlanStep enrichment (required_inputs -> params, output -> output_schema, required_context, avg_latency_ms -> timeout_ms). **Meta-agent** (§11.1.5): EXPAND may call discover twice -- once for agent contracts, once for tools to populate AgentSpec.tools_granted[]. **Timeout**: 50ms. **Retry**: 1 (no double-count). **On failure**: return empty RetrievalResult, caller proceeds with degraded input. **Latency target** (§11.1.6): <20ms for 10K caps, <50ms for 100K caps. Canonical path always issues call; skip optimization is V2 enhancement. **EXPAND usage**: typically none -- uses SKETCH results via SketchResult passthrough. **Timeout**: 10ms. **Retry**: 0 (lock-free read, failure unlikely). **On failure**: use PlanRequest.context snapshot as fallback. **Latency target**: <10ms (in-process, lock-free). |

If `is_available() == false` (K0_OFFLINE) -> return `BridgeCommandResult.fail("k0_offline", ...)` immediately, do NOT count against PLAN-05 (no actual call made). If `get_health().mode == K0_DEGRADED` -> dispatch with extended timeout_ms=200ms (instead of 100ms). If K0_FULL -> normal dispatch, timeout_ms=100ms. **Offline degradation** (§11.4.4): plan based only on Fabric capabilities + SessionState + PlanRequest.intent. Valid but less personalized -- no historical preferences, prior outcomes, or long-term facts. Personalization is additive, not structural. **Caller** (§11.4.6): SKETCH only (3rd of 6 calls). EXPAND does NOT call recall. **Timeout**: 100ms (K0_FULL), 200ms (K0_DEGRADED). **Retry**: 0. **On failure**: return empty BridgeCommandResult.fail(), SKETCH proceeds without long-term memory. |

If false -> raise BudgetExhaustedError (log warning with tool_name and current count, include trace_id). (3) Check `tool_name in _ROUTING_TABLE`. If false -> raise UnknownToolError. (4) Dispatch to port method (async). (5) Increment `_tool_call_count += 1`. (6) Return result. **Budget allocation per stage** (§11.5.2): SKETCH uses 3 calls (discover_capabilities + query_planning_context + recall_for_planning). EXPAND uses 2-3 calls (discover_capabilities refined + find_relevant_prompts + optional 3rd). VALIDATE uses 0 (IFabricRetrievalPort directly, NOT via ToolCallRouter). COMMIT uses 0. **Counter lifecycle**: reset at LC_PLAN_START via `router.reset()` called by PipelineController. Reset at micro-replan start (abbreviated pipeline gets fresh 6-call budget, §10.3). NEVER reset mid-pipeline between SKETCH and EXPAND. **Retry accounting** (§11.5.3): retries do NOT double-count. Counter increments ONCE per logical call attempt (not per network request). If call 1 fails and retries once, tool_call_count increments by 1 (not 2). **K0 offline exception** (§11.4.3): when K0 is offline and recall skipped via `is_available()` pre-check, counter is NOT incremented (no actual call dispatched). **Private helpers**: `_check_budget(self) -> None` raises BudgetExhaustedError at limit. `_check_tool_name(self, tool_name: str) -> None` raises UnknownToolError for unknown tools. |

Zero side effects. No action, execute, write, or mutate path exists in the ToolCallRouter. **Structural guarantee layers**: (1) Routing table is a class-level frozen constant -- no dynamic route registration possible (no `register_tool()` or `add_route()` methods). (2) Table contains ONLY 4 entries: discover_capabilities, find_relevant_prompts, query_planning_context, recall_for_planning. (3) Port interfaces accessed through ToolCallRouter have NO write methods: IFabricRetrievalPort has discovery-only methods (no `invoke_capability`), IStateReadPort has `read_sections` / `get_snapshot` only (no write), IBridgePort.query() is read-only recall (persist_plan is used by CommitService directly, NOT through ToolCallRouter). **PLAN-06 enforcement**: Planner never executes capabilities. ToolCallRouter has NO execute route -- discover_capabilities finds capabilities but does NOT invoke them. This is a structural guarantee (no code path from ToolCallRouter to capability execution). **Test enforcement**: test that `_ROUTING_TABLE` contains exactly 4 entries and no others. Test that no dynamic registration methods exist. Test that `call()` with unknown tool_name raises UnknownToolError. Test that routing table maps only to read-only port methods. **Contract**: ToolCallRouter module.contract.yaml declares tool_names as frozen set of exactly 4 read-only tools. |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File location**: `k1/planner/services/tool_call_router.py` [F25]. Single file contains ToolCallRouter class.
> - **Dependency layer**: Layer 2 (§30.6). Imports from Layer 1 (IFabricRetrievalPort, IStateReadPort, IBridgePort), Layer 0 (RetrievalResult, BridgeCommandResult, SessionSnapshot, ToolCallStatus, BudgetExhaustedError, UnknownToolError, PlannerConfig).
> - **Consumers**: SketchService (3 calls: discover + context + recall via convenience methods), ExpandService (2-3 calls: discover refined + prompts + optional). NOT consumed by ValidateService (uses IFabricRetrievalPort directly) or CommitService (no tool calls).
> - **Counter lifecycle**: PipelineController calls `router.reset()` at LC_PLAN_START and micro-replan start. PipelineController reads `router.tool_call_count` for stage delta emission and passes to ValidateService for PLAN-05 post-hoc validation (issue 3.3.4).
> - **Testing**: ~35 tests (§17.7): routing correctness (~8), call counting PLAN-05 (~8), degraded paths (~8), concurrent calls (~5), input validation (~3), cross-stage reset (~3).
> - **Anti-hallucination**: ToolCallRouter is a thin, deterministic dispatch layer. It does NOT make LLM calls. It does NOT interpret tool results. It does NOT make routing decisions based on content. All routing is static (class-level constant table). Its only logic is: validate tool name, check budget, dispatch to port, increment counter, return result. Do NOT add content-based routing, LLM tool-use patterns, or dynamic tool registration.

### Epic 4.2: HILCoordinator

**Goal**: Implement the HILCoordinator service that manages exactly 2 Human-in-the-Loop interaction points (clarification during SKETCH, approval during VALIDATE), enforces PLAN-10 round budget (max 2 rounds per plan), generates natural-language HIL messages via ILLMPort, publishes/subscribes to 4 HIL event topics via IEventPort, correlates responses by request_id, and provides timeout-based fallback paths. ~35 tests.

> **Reference**: planner.md §12 (HIL System Deep Dive), §12.1 (Namespace Split -- planning-time vs execution-time HIL events), §12.2 (Requirement Clarification -- trigger, flow, event schema, round budget, 60s timeout), §12.3 (Plan Approval -- trigger, flow, event schema, response routing, modification handling), §12.4 (PENDING_CLARIFICATIONS -- Concierge correlation map), §12.5 (HILCoordinator Internal Design -- state management, concurrency contract, timeout handling), §12.6 (Error Recovery Summary), §13.1 (LLM Call Matrix -- LLM-5a clarification 300 tokens, LLM-5b approval 400 tokens), §17.8 (HILCoordinator -- ~35 tests, constructor, test categories).
>
> - **Constructor** (§17.8): `HILCoordinator(llm_port: ILLMPort, event_port: IEventPort)`.
> - **Ports used**: ILLMPort (generate natural-language questions and plan summaries), IEventPort (publish outbound HIL events, subscribe to inbound response events).
> - **Invariant enforced**: PLAN-10 (max 2 HIL clarification rounds per plan, then best-effort).
> - **4 event topics** (§12.1): outbound: `k1.hil.clarification.v1`, `k1.hil.approval_request.v1`. Inbound: `k1.hil.clarification_response.v1`, `k1.hil.approval_response.v1`. All planning-time events, distinct from execution-time HIL events owned by Orchestrator.
> - **State** (§12.5.1): `round_count: int`, `pending_request_id: Optional[str]`, `hil_type: Optional[str]`, `waiting: bool`. All reset at LC_PLAN_START.
> - **Concurrency** (§12.5.2): only ONE HIL interaction active at a time per plan. Clarification (SKETCH) completes before approval (VALIDATE). Sequential pipeline, no overlap.
> - **LLM usage** (§13.1): clarification question generation (CHAT, 300 tokens, 3000ms, temp 0.7), approval summary generation (CHAT, 400 tokens, 3000ms, temp 0.7). Both WITHIN their parent stage's token allocation.
> - **PLAN-04 interaction** (§12.2.4): HIL wait time is EXCLUDED from the 45s planning budget. 45s covers computational planning only (LLM + tool calls + assembly). Orchestrator CB_PLANNER timeout extends when HIL active.

HILCoordinator is the only service with mutable per-plan state (alongside ToolCallRouter). **Public interface**: `async def request_clarification(self, request_id: str, question_context: Dict[str, Any]) -> Optional[str]` -- triggers clarification flow, returns user response text or None on timeout. Called by SketchService when ambiguity detected. `async def request_approval(self, request_id: str, expanded_plan: ExpandedPlan, arbiter_verdict: ValidationVerdict) -> ApprovalResponse` -- triggers approval flow, returns ApprovalResponse (approve/modify/reject). Called by ValidateService after arbiter approves. `def reset(self) -> None` -- reset all state (called by PipelineController at LC_PLAN_START). `@property def round_count(self) -> int` -- current round count (read by PipelineController for stage deltas). **ApprovalResponse** (named tuple or dataclass): `ApprovalResponse(response_type: str, modifications: Optional[Dict])` where response_type is "approve"/"modify"/"reject". **Dependency layer**: Layer 2 (§30.6) -- imports Layer 1 (ILLMPort, IEventPort), Layer 0 (ExpandedPlan, ValidationVerdict, PlanRequest, StageContext, HubRequest, HubResponse, HILClarificationPayload, HILApprovalRequestPayload, ApprovalResponse, HILBudgetExceededError). |
| 4.2.2 | Clarification flow (event emit + response wait) | TODO | k1/planner/services/hil_coordinator.py [F26] | **Flow** (§12.2.2, 7 steps): (1) SketchService calls `hil_coord.request_clarification(request_id, question_context)` with question_context containing intent, discovered_caps, session_context, recall_results. (2) HILCoordinator checks round budget: if `_round_count >= 2` -> return None (PLAN-10 exhausted, proceed best-effort). (3) Generate question via ILLMPort (see issue 4.2.6 for LLM details). (4) Emit event via IEventPort: topic=`k1.hil.clarification.v1`, payload=`HILClarificationPayload(request_id, plan_id=None, question=<LLM-generated>, context_hint=<known context summary>, round=_round_count+1, max_rounds=2)`. (5) Set `_pending_request_id=request_id`, `_hil_type="clarification"`, `_waiting=True`. (6) Wait for response: subscribe to `k1.hil.clarification_response.v1`, correlate by request_id, timeout=60s. On response: extract user_response text. (7) Increment `_round_count += 1`. Set `_waiting=False`. Return user_response to SketchService. **Multi-round** (§12.2.2 step 6): if answer insufficient AND round < 2, SketchService may call request_clarification again with refined question_context incorporating first response. Second call uses round=2. **Concierge routing** (§12.4): Event Bus -> Concierge receives event -> writes to PENDING_CLARIFICATIONS map keyed by `{request_id, originator: "planner"}` -> renders to user -> user responds -> HIL Response Detector matches and routes as `k1.hil.clarification_response.v1` (not new turn). **Multi-round concatenation**: if 2 rounds occurred, SketchService concatenates both responses in HIL_ADDENDUM prompt slot. |
| 4.2.3 | Approval flow (event emit + response wait) | TODO | k1/planner/services/hil_coordinator.py [F26] | **Flow** (§12.3.2, 5 steps): (1) ValidateService calls `hil_coord.request_approval(request_id, expanded_plan, arbiter_verdict)`. (2) Generate plan summary via ILLMPort (CHAT, 400 tokens, 3000ms -- see issue 4.2.6). Prompt includes PLAN_STEPS (serialized), SIDE_EFFECTS (steps with has_side_effects=true), SAFETY_ASSESSMENT, COST_ESTIMATE (sum of step cost_per_call). (3) Emit event via IEventPort: topic=`k1.hil.approval_request.v1`, payload=`HILApprovalRequestPayload(request_id, plan_id=None, summary=<LLM-formatted>, options=["approve","modify","reject"], side_effects=[{step_id, capability, description} per side-effect step], safety_assessment=<arbiter assessment>, estimated_duration_ms=<plan estimate>)`. (4) Set `_pending_request_id=request_id`, `_hil_type="approval"`, `_waiting=True`. Wait for `k1.hil.approval_response.v1`, correlate by request_id, timeout=120s (longer than clarification -- user needs review time). (5) On response: parse `response_type` ("approve"/"modify"/"reject") + optional `modifications` dict. Set `_waiting=False`. Return `ApprovalResponse(response_type, modifications)`. **Response routing** (§12.3.4): "approve" -> ValidateService proceeds to COMMIT. "modify" -> apply field-level overrides per step_id, re-run deterministic checks (DAG may change from step removal), proceed to COMMIT (no additional arbiter call -- user approval supersedes). "reject" -> PLAN FAILED. **Modification protocol** (§12.3.5): modifications keyed by step_id. `{step_id: {params: {...}}}` for param override. `{step_id: {remove: true}}` for step removal (descendants with only this dep get deps=[]). Re-run deterministic checks after mods; if fail -> PLAN FAILED. |
| 4.2.4 | Round count enforcement (PLAN-10, max 2 rounds) | TODO | k1/planner/services/hil_coordinator.py [F26] | **PLAN-10** (§12.2.4): max 2 clarification rounds per plan, then best-effort. **Round budget accounting**: `_round_count` tracks clarification rounds only (approval is a single interaction, not multi-round). On each `request_clarification()` call: (1) Check `_round_count < max_rounds` (default 2 from PlannerConfig.max_hil_rounds). If >= max -> return None immediately (do NOT generate question, do NOT emit event). Log info "hil_round_budget_exhausted, proceeding_best_effort". (2) After successful response or timeout: increment `_round_count += 1`. **Approval does NOT count**: approval is not counted against the round budget (it is a different interaction type with its own timeout). PLAN-10 governs clarification rounds only. **Counter lifecycle**: `_round_count` reset to 0 at LC_PLAN_START via `hil_coord.reset()` called by PipelineController. **Maximum HIL wall time** (§12.2.4): 2 rounds * 60s clarification + 120s approval = 240s worst case. This is EXCLUDED from PLAN-04's 45s computational budget. **Micro-replan** (PLAN-12, §10.4): micro-replan NEVER triggers HIL. If called during micro-replan context, request_clarification and request_approval should short-circuit and return None / auto-approve respectively. Detection: `ctx.is_micro_replan` flag or separate micro-method that skips HIL. **Private helper**: `_check_round_budget(self) -> bool` returns True if rounds available, False if exhausted. |
| 4.2.5 | Timeout handling & fallback | TODO | k1/planner/services/hil_coordinator.py [F26] | **Clarification timeout** (§12.5.3, 60s per round): `_wait_for_response(timeout_ms=60000)` expires -> set `_waiting=False` -> return None to SketchService -> SketchService proceeds with best-effort interpretation. Prompt includes note "(User clarification unavailable -- proceeding with best-effort interpretation based on available context)". NOT a stage failure. **Approval timeout** (§12.5.3, 120s): `_wait_for_response(timeout_ms=120000)` expires -> set `_waiting=False` -> ValidateService evaluates auto-approve safety: IF all side-effecting steps have `safety_band_min == "GREEN"` AND arbiter `safety_assessment == "safe"` -> auto-approve, proceed to COMMIT. ELSE -> PLAN FAILED (unsafe steps require explicit approval). Return `ApprovalResponse(response_type="approve/reject", modifications=None)` based on safety evaluation. **LLM failure during question/summary generation** (§12.5.3): -> skip HIL interaction entirely. Clarification: proceed without clarification (degraded plan quality). Approval: auto-approve if safe, otherwise FAIL. Do NOT retry LLM for HIL messages. **Event Bus delivery failure** (§12.6): HILCoordinator never receives response -> timeout path triggers. Resilient by design. **Concierge miss** (§12.6): if PENDING_CLARIFICATIONS map fails to match, response treated as new turn, HILCoordinator times out -> fallback path. **Private methods**: `async def _wait_for_response(self, topic: str, request_id: str, timeout_ms: int) -> Optional[Dict]` -- subscribes to inbound topic, correlates by request_id, returns payload or None on timeout. `def _evaluate_auto_approve(self, expanded_plan: ExpandedPlan, verdict: ValidationVerdict) -> bool` -- returns True if safe to auto-approve. |

Keep it conversational and appropriate for a family context."). USER = INTENT (PlanRequest.intent) + KNOWN_CONTEXT (summary of what Planner already knows from session context + K0 recall) + AMBIGUITY_SIGNAL (what specifically is unclear). Output: ~100-200 token natural-language question. **300-token budget is WITHIN SKETCH stage allocation** (not additional). **Approval summary generation** (§12.3.2 step 1, LLM-5b per §13.1): `HubRequest{capability: CHAT, constraints: {max_tokens: 400, timeout_ms: 3000, temperature: 0.7, consumer_id: "planner"}, trace_id}`. **Prompt**: SYSTEM = ROLE_DEFINITION ("You are a family assistant presenting a plan for approval") + OUTPUT_CONTRACT ("Summarize the plan in plain language. Highlight side effects, costs, and affected external systems. Present options: approve, modify, or reject."). USER = PLAN_STEPS (serialized step list) + SIDE_EFFECTS (has_side_effects=true steps with descriptions) + SAFETY_ASSESSMENT (arbiter field) + COST_ESTIMATE (sum of cost_per_call). Output: ~200-300 token plan summary. **400-token budget is WITHIN VALIDATE stage allocation**. **Private methods**: `async def _generate_clarification_question(self, question_context: Dict, trace_id: str) -> Optional[str]` -- returns question text or None on LLM failure. `async def _generate_approval_summary(self, expanded_plan, verdict, trace_id: str) -> Optional[str]` -- returns summary text or None on LLM failure. On LLM failure: skip HIL entirely (§12.5.3). |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File location**: `k1/planner/services/hil_coordinator.py` [F26]. Single file contains HILCoordinator class + ApprovalResponse type.
> - **Dependency layer**: Layer 2 (§30.6). Imports from Layer 1 (ILLMPort, IEventPort), Layer 0 (ExpandedPlan, ValidationVerdict, PlanRequest, StageContext, HubRequest, HubResponse, HILClarificationPayload, HILApprovalRequestPayload, ApprovalResponse, HILBudgetExceededError, PlannerConfig).
> - **Consumers**: SketchService calls `request_clarification()` (0-2 times per plan). ValidateService calls `request_approval()` (0-1 times per plan). PipelineController calls `reset()` at LC_PLAN_START and reads `round_count` for stage deltas.
> - **Event routing**: Outbound events route via IEventPort -> K1 Event Bus -> Concierge. Concierge writes PENDING_CLARIFICATIONS map (`{request_id, originator: "planner"}`), renders to user. User responds -> Concierge HIL Response Detector matches request_id -> emits inbound response event -> IEventPort delivers to HILCoordinator.
> - **Testing**: ~35 tests (§17.8): clarification round-trip (~8), approval round-trip (~8), round budget PLAN-10 (~5), timeout handling (~5), event correlation (~5), edge cases (duplicate/out-of-order/unknown request_id, ~4).
> - **Anti-hallucination**: HILCoordinator does NOT write to SessionState (PLAN-01), does NOT execute capabilities (PLAN-06), does NOT perform discovery tool calls (no ToolCallRouter reference). Its only I/O is: (1) ILLMPort.execute() for question/summary generation (2 CHAT calls max), (2) IEventPort.publish() for outbound HIL events, (3) IEventPort.subscribe() for inbound response events. Do NOT add tool routing, capability execution, or direct user I/O to this service.

> - [ ] Mutable per-plan state: `_round_count: int`, `_pending_request_id: Optional[str]`, `_hil_type: Optional[str]`, `_waiting: bool` -- all reset at LC_PLAN_START
> - [ ] `async request_clarification(request_id, question_context) -> Optional[str]` -- returns user response or None on timeout/budget exhausted
> - [ ] Clarification flow (SS12.2.2, 7 steps): check round budget -> generate question via ILLMPort(CHAT, 300 tokens, 3000ms, temp 0.7) -> emit k1.hil.clarification.v1 -> wait 60s for k1.hil.clarification_response.v1 -> correlate by request_id -> return response

> - [ ] Modification protocol (SS12.3.5): modifications keyed by step_id, support param override and step removal; re-run deterministic checks after mods
