## Milestone 5: Micro-Replan

> **Goal**: Implement the abbreviated 3-stage micro-replan pipeline (SKETCH -> EXPAND -> VALIDATE, no COMMIT).

### Epic 5.1: Micro-Replan Pipeline

**Goal**: Implement the abbreviated 3-stage micro-replan pipeline (MICRO_SKETCH -> MICRO_EXPAND -> MICRO_VALIDATE -> COMMIT) orchestrated by PipelineController, triggered synchronously by Orchestrator's MicroReplanCheckpoint guard (ORCH-13), scoped to replacement of remaining steps only (PLAN-12), with 10s total budget, zero HIL interactions, max 1 per DAG execution, and graceful fallback (return None -> Orchestrator continues original plan). 8 issues covering trigger detection, 3 abbreviated stages, overlap heuristic, PLAN-12 enforcement, timeout, and 3 dedicated FSM states.

> **Reference**: planner.md §10 (Micro-Replan Deep Dive), §10.1 (Protocol -- invocation path, delivery protocol, FSM states), §10.2 (Inputs -- MicroReplanRequest, field details, overlap heuristic), §10.3 (Abbreviated Pipeline -- comparison table, Micro-SKETCH/EXPAND/VALIDATE/COMMIT, timing budget), §10.4 (Output -- partial CommittedPlan, merge protocol), §10.5 (Guard Rails -- PLAN-12, max replans, timeout fallback, no HIL, token budget), §10.6 (Error Recovery -- failure scenarios, no revise loop, stage delta), §18.3 (FSM Micro-Replan State Diagram), §18.4 (Transition Table -- MICRO_SKETCH/MICRO_EXPAND/MICRO_VALIDATE transitions).
>
> - **Entry point** (§10.1.2): `IPlannerMailbox.micro_replan(MicroReplanRequest) -> Optional[CommittedPlan]`. Synchronous (blocking, direct return). Bypasses mailbox queue. Acquires `_plan_lock`.
> - **Pipeline flow** (§10.1.3): IDLE -> MICRO_SKETCH -> MICRO_EXPAND -> MICRO_VALIDATE -> COMMITTING -> COMPLETED -> IDLE. COMMITTING and COMPLETED states are shared with full pipeline (same CommitService).
> - **Input** (§10.2): `MicroReplanRequest(original_plan_id, completed_results: Dict[str, StepResult], remaining_steps: List[PlanStep], trace_id, request_id, discoveries: List[Discovery], failure_context: Optional[FailureContext])`. Planner receives what succeeded (completed_results), what was planned (remaining_steps), what changed (discoveries), and why (failure_context).
> - **Token budgets** (§10.3.6, §10.5.5): Micro-SKETCH={max_tokens: 1024, timeout_ms: 5000, temp: 0.7}, Micro-EXPAND={max_tokens: 512, timeout_ms: 3000, temp: 0.3}, Micro-VALIDATE={max_tokens: 256, timeout_ms: 2000, temp: 0.1}, COMMIT=0 tokens. Total ~1.8K tokens within ~2K budget.
> - **Key differences from full pipeline** (§10.3.1): 10s total (vs 45s), ~2K tokens (vs ~3.5K), up to 3 tool calls (vs 6), no HIL (vs clarification+approval), scope is replacement steps only (vs full plan from intent), delivery is synchronous return (vs event bus).
> - **Error recovery** (§10.6): ALL failures -> return None to Orchestrator -> continue original plan. No retry of any micro-stage. Verdict "revise" treated as "approved" (best-effort). Verdict "reject" -> return None.

| 5.1.1 | Micro-replan trigger detection | TODO | k1/planner/pipeline_controller.py [F03] | **Entry point** (§10.1.2): `PipelineController.micro_replan(request: MicroReplanRequest) -> Optional[CommittedPlan]`. Called from `MailboxAdapter.micro_replan()` which acquires `_plan_lock` first. **Trigger flow** (§10.1.1, Orchestrator side -- already implemented): DAGExecutor wave N completes -> MicroReplanCheckpoint.after_wave() -> extract discoveries from CapabilityResult.data["discoveries"] -> check overlap heuristic (§10.2.2) -> if overlap AND replans_used < max_replans -> build MicroReplanRequest -> `await asyncio.wait_for(planner.micro_replan(request), timeout=10.0)`. **Planner-side detection**: PipelineController receives MicroReplanRequest and recognizes it as micro-replan (distinct type from PlanRequest). **Setup sequence**: (1) Validate MicroReplanRequest: original_plan_id non-empty, remaining_steps non-empty, trace_id non-empty. (2) Reset counters: `_tool_router.reset()` (fresh 6-call budget for micro-replan, §10.3), `_hil_coord.reset()` (reset round count, though HIL unused in micro). (3) FSM transition: IDLE -> MICRO_SKETCH via `micro_start` trigger (§18.4). (4) Create StageContext with micro-replan budgets from PlannerConfig. (5) Emit delta: `{type: "micro_replan_started", original_plan_id, remaining_step_count, discovery_count, trigger: "discovery"/"failure"}`. **Return contract**: `Optional[CommittedPlan]` -- None if any stage fails (Orchestrator continues original plan). CommittedPlan contains only replacement steps. |
| 5.1.2 | Abbreviated SKETCH stage (micro context) | TODO | k1/planner/stages/sketch_service.py [F19] | **Micro-SKETCH** (§10.3.2): `SketchService.micro_execute(request: MicroReplanRequest, ctx: StageContext) -> SketchResult`. Reuses SketchService but with abbreviated inputs and reduced budget. **Budget**: {max_tokens: 1024, timeout_ms: 5000, temperature: 0.7} -- half the tokens and timeout of full SKETCH (§10.3.6). **Prompt composition** (§10.3.2): SYSTEM = ROLE_DEFINITION (micro-replan task: re-plan remaining portion) + OUTPUT_SCHEMA (MicroSketchResult JSON Schema: replacement_steps[], reasoning, reuse_steps[]). USER = ORIGINAL_INTENT (from original PlanRequest.intent), COMPLETED_SUMMARY (step_id + capability + status + key outputs from MicroReplanRequest.completed_results), REMAINING_STEPS (uncompleted steps needing replacement/modification), DISCOVERIES (new info that invalidated remaining steps), FAILURE_CONTEXT (if present: failed step_id, error_code, error_message, partial_result). **Key differences from full SKETCH**: (1) No `query_planning_context` or `recall_for_planning` tool calls -- completed_results provides sufficient context. (2) At most 1 `discover_capabilities` call if LLM determines new capabilities needed for replacement steps (vs 3 calls in full SKETCH). Tool call count: 0-1 of 3 budget. (3) No HIL clarification (PLAN-12, §10.5.4 -- micro-replan never triggers HIL). (4) `reuse_steps` field in output allows LLM to indicate which remaining_steps are still valid (minimize disruption). **Error recovery** (§10.6.1): LLM failure -> no retry in micro-replan (time budget too tight). Return None, PipelineController sets FAILED, returns None to Orchestrator. **FSM**: MICRO_SKETCH -> MICRO_EXPAND on success, MICRO_SKETCH -> FAILED on LLM error. |
| 5.1.3 | Abbreviated EXPAND stage (micro context) | TODO | k1/planner/stages/expand_service.py [F20] | **Micro-EXPAND** (§10.3.3): `ExpandService.micro_execute(micro_sketch_result: SketchResult, completed_results: Dict[str, StepResult], ctx: StageContext) -> ExpandedPlan`. Reuses ExpandService but scoped to replacement steps only. **Budget**: {max_tokens: 512, timeout_ms: 3000, temperature: 0.3} -- half the tokens and timeout of full EXPAND (§10.3.6). **Prompt composition** (§10.3.3): SKETCH_PLAN (MicroSketchResult.replacement_steps only, NOT full plan), COMPLETED_CONTEXT (available outputs from completed steps for param binding -- completed_results[step_id].result.data), CAPABILITY_SET (cached from full plan discovery or 1 new discover_capabilities call), DEPENDENCY_RULES (must reference completed step IDs for cross-boundary deps). **Cross-boundary dependencies** (§10.3.3): replacement steps CAN depend on completed steps via `deps: ["s1"]` where s1 is already completed. Orchestrator resolves these from completed_results when merging. Inter-step references like `$s1.result.data.field` may reference completed step outputs. **Post-LLM enrichment**: same deterministic enrichment as full EXPAND (7 of 14 PlanStep fields from CapabilityContract). **No revise loop** (§10.6.2): unlike full pipeline, micro-replan has NO revise loop between VALIDATE and EXPAND. Single attempt per stage. **Error recovery**: LLM failure -> no retry, no fallback to degraded PlanSteps. Return None. **Tool calls**: 0-1 additional discover_capabilities (from remaining tool budget). Total after MICRO_EXPAND: 0-2 of 3. **FSM**: MICRO_EXPAND -> MICRO_VALIDATE on success, MICRO_EXPAND -> FAILED on LLM error. |
| 5.1.4 | Abbreviated VALIDATE stage (micro context) | TODO | k1/planner/stages/validate_service.py [F21] | **Micro-VALIDATE** (§10.3.4): `ValidateService.micro_execute(expanded_plan: ExpandedPlan, ctx: StageContext) -> ValidationVerdict`. Reuses ValidateService but with tightest budget and simplified verdict handling. **Budget**: {max_tokens: 256, timeout_ms: 2000, temperature: 0.1} -- tightest of all stages (§10.3.6). **Deterministic checks** (§10.3.4): same as full VALIDATE (DAG cycle detection via Kahn's algorithm on replacement steps + cross-boundary deps to completed steps, capability existence check). **LLM arbiter**: same ValidationVerdict structure (status: approved/revise/reject) but with simplified routing: verdict "revise" treated as "approved" (best-effort -- §10.6.2), verdict "reject" -> micro-replan fails (return None to Orchestrator, continue original plan), verdict "approved" -> proceed to COMMIT. **No revise loop** (§10.6.2): single attempt, no loop back to EXPAND. Time budget too tight within 10s total. **No HIL approval** (§10.5.4): micro-replan never triggers HIL. Original plan already had approval; replacement steps inherit that approval (same intent, user already approved overall approach). **Cross-boundary validation**: DAG check must account for deps referencing completed step IDs (these are valid even though those steps are not in expanded_plan.steps -- they exist in completed_results). **Error recovery**: arbiter timeout -> auto-approve if deterministic checks passed (same as full VALIDATE §8.6 Path 2). Arbiter "reject" -> return None. **FSM**: MICRO_VALIDATE -> COMMITTING on approved/"revise", MICRO_VALIDATE -> FAILED on "reject". |
| 5.1.5 | Discovery overlap heuristic | TODO | k1/planner/pipeline_controller.py [F03] | **Overlap heuristic** (§10.2.2): implemented in MicroReplanCheckpoint (Orchestrator side, already exists) but Planner-side revalidation is needed for defense-in-depth. PipelineController may re-evaluate overlap before committing to the full micro-replan pipeline. **Algorithm** (§10.2.2, 3-way field match): for each discovery in MicroReplanRequest.discoveries, for each step in remaining_steps, for each param_name in step.params: (1) exact match (case-insensitive): `discovery.field.lower() == param_name.lower()`, (2) substring forward: `discovery.field.lower() in param_name.lower()`, (3) substring reverse: `param_name.lower() in discovery.field.lower()`. ANY match -> overlap detected -> micro-replan is warranted. **Example** (§10.2.2): step s1 discovers `{field: "restaurant_closed", value: true}`, remaining step s3 has params `{restaurant_name: "Luigi's"}`. Substring: "restaurant" in both -> overlap. **Performance**: O(D *S* P) where D = discoveries, S = remaining steps, P = avg params per step. For typical plans (D=1-3, S=2-5, P=3-5): <1ms (ORCH-13 P99 target). **Planner-side check** (defense-in-depth): `_validate_overlap(self, request: MicroReplanRequest) -> bool`. If no overlap detected on Planner side: log info "micro_replan_no_overlap_on_recheck", still proceed (Orchestrator already validated -- trust caller). This is advisory, not blocking. **Edge cases**: empty discoveries list -> no overlap (should not reach Planner -- Orchestrator skips at step 2 of §10.1.1). Empty remaining_steps -> invalid MicroReplanRequest (rejected at validation in 5.1.1). |
| 5.1.6 | Remaining-steps-only adjustment (PLAN-12) | TODO | k1/planner/pipeline_controller.py [F03] | **PLAN-12** (§10.5.1): micro-replan adjusts remaining steps ONLY. Completed steps are FROZEN -- cannot be modified, removed, or re-executed. **Enforcement layers** (§10.5.1): (1) MicroReplanCheckpoint (Orchestrator) passes only `remaining_steps` (unexecuted) to Planner -- completed steps never enter PlannerRequest. (2) MicroReplanRequest type carries `remaining_steps: List[PlanStep]` (uncompleted only) and `completed_results: Dict[str, StepResult]` (read-only context). (3) Micro-SKETCH prompt includes instruction: "Do NOT modify or reference completed steps as replaceable. Completed steps are frozen." (4) DAGExecutor merge protocol (§10.4.1): keeps all completed_results, discards original remaining_steps, inserts replacement steps, rebuilds wave schedule from new dependencies. **Planner-side enforcement**: `_enforce_plan12(self, replacement_plan: ExpandedPlan, completed_ids: Set[str]) -> ExpandedPlan`. Validates: (a) no replacement step.id overlaps with any completed step_id, (b) replacement step deps referencing completed step_ids are valid (those steps exist in completed_results), (c) no replacement step attempts to undo/redo a completed step. On violation: log error, strip offending references, proceed with cleaned plan (defense-in-depth, should not happen if prompt instructs correctly). **Output contract** (§10.4.2): CommittedPlan.steps contains ONLY replacement steps. CommittedPlan.dependencies may reference completed step_ids (cross-boundary deps). CommittedPlan.plan_id is fresh uuid4 (distinct from original_plan_id). |
| 5.1.7 | 10s timeout enforcement | TODO | k1/planner/pipeline_controller.py [F03] | **Total budget** (§10.3.6): 10s synchronous timeout (hardcoded in Orchestrator's PlannerAdapter and MicroReplanCheckpoint). PipelineController does NOT enforce the outer 10s -- that is the caller's `asyncio.wait_for(timeout=10.0)`. PipelineController enforces per-stage budgets within StageContext. **Per-stage timing** (§10.3.6): Micro-SKETCH timeout_ms=5000, Micro-EXPAND timeout_ms=3000, Micro-VALIDATE timeout_ms=2000, COMMIT <100ms. Theoretical max ~10.1s (barely fits). Practical p50 ~8s, p99 ~15s (may exceed -> caller timeout -> graceful fallback). **StageContext injection**: PipelineController creates StageContext for each micro-stage with `timeout_remaining_ms` computed from: `micro_replan_start_time + 10000 - now()`. Progressive squeeze: MICRO_SKETCH gets ~5s, MICRO_EXPAND gets remainder after SKETCH (~3s), MICRO_VALIDATE gets remainder (~2s). If timeout_remaining_ms <= 0 before a stage starts: skip remaining stages, return None. **Caller-side timeout** (§10.5.3): `asyncio.wait_for(planner.micro_replan(request), timeout=10.0)`. On timeout: MicroReplanCheckpoint returns GuardDecision(action=CONTINUE, reason="Planner micro_replan timed out after 10.0s"). DAGExecutor continues with original remaining steps. No error propagation, no plan failure, log warning only. **On Planner returns None** (§10.5.3): same CONTINUE behavior -- Planner may return None if it determines remaining steps are still valid or if any stage fails. **On Planner exception**: MicroReplanCheckpoint catches, logs warning, returns CONTINUE. CB_PLANNER may trip on accumulated failures. **PlannerConfig field**: `micro_replan_timeout_ms: int = 10000`. |
| 5.1.8 | Plan FSM micro-replan states (MICRO_SKETCH, MICRO_EXPAND, MICRO_VALIDATE) | TODO | k1/planner/plan_fsm.py [F04] | **3 dedicated FSM states** (§18.1, §18.3): MICRO_SKETCH, MICRO_EXPAND, MICRO_VALIDATE. Added to PlanState enum alongside the 7 existing states (IDLE, SKETCHING, EXPANDING, VALIDATING, COMMITTING, COMPLETED, FAILED, CANCELLED = 10 total with micro states). **State diagram** (§18.3): IDLE -> MICRO_SKETCH (trigger: micro_start, guard: plan lock acquired) -> MICRO_EXPAND (trigger: stage_complete, guard: no cancel pending) -> MICRO_VALIDATE (trigger: stage_complete, guard: no cancel pending) -> COMMITTING (trigger: verdict approved or "revise" treated as approved) -> COMPLETED (trigger: commit_complete) -> IDLE (trigger: lock released). **Transition table** (§18.4): MICRO_SKETCH -> MICRO_EXPAND (micro-SketchService returns), MICRO_SKETCH -> FAILED (LLM failure, no retry). MICRO_EXPAND -> MICRO_VALIDATE (micro-ExpandService returns), MICRO_EXPAND -> FAILED (LLM failure, no retry). MICRO_VALIDATE -> COMMITTING (verdict approved or "revise" treated as approved), MICRO_VALIDATE -> FAILED (verdict "reject" -> return None). COMMITTING and COMPLETED are SHARED with full pipeline (same CommitService, same transitions). **Cancel during micro-replan** (§18.5.3): cancel flag checked between micro-stages via `ctx.cancel_check()`. On cancel: FSM -> CANCELLED -> IDLE, return None to Orchestrator. **Illegal transitions**: any micro-state -> any non-successor state raises IllegalStateTransitionError. **Delta emissions**: each micro-state transition emits `{type: "stage_transition", from: "MICRO_SKETCH", to: "MICRO_EXPAND"}`. **On FAILED** (§10.6.1): return None to Orchestrator (not plan.failed.v1 event -- micro-replan failure is not reported as plan failure, it is graceful degradation). |

> **Wiring**:
>
> - **File locations**: Issues 5.1.1, 5.1.5, 5.1.6, 5.1.7 modify `k1/planner/pipeline_controller.py` [F03] (PipelineController.micro_replan method, overlap validation, PLAN-12 enforcement, timeout management). Issues 5.1.2, 5.1.3, 5.1.4 modify stage services [F19, F20, F21] (micro_execute methods added in M3). Issue 5.1.8 modifies `k1/planner/plan_fsm.py` [F04] (3 new PlanState enum values + transition rules).
> - **Dependency on M2**: PipelineController (Epic 2.2) owns the `micro_replan()` method skeleton and FSM state management. M5 fills the micro-replan-specific logic within that method.
> - **Dependency on M3**: Stage services (Epics 3.1-3.3) define `micro_execute()` method signatures. M5 fills the micro-specific prompt assembly, budget injection, and error recovery within those methods.
> - **Dependency on M4**: ToolCallRouter (Epic 4.1) counter is reset at micro-replan start (`router.reset()`). HILCoordinator (Epic 4.2) is reset but NOT invoked (micro-replan never triggers HIL).
> - **Input type**: MicroReplanRequest from `k1/orchestrator/types.py` (defined in M1, Epic 1.2, issue 1.2.9). Contains completed_results (Dict[str, StepResult]), remaining_steps (List[PlanStep]), discoveries (List[Discovery]), failure_context (Optional[FailureContext]).
> - **Output**: `Optional[CommittedPlan]` -- None on any failure (Orchestrator continues original plan). CommittedPlan contains ONLY replacement steps. Delivery is synchronous return (NOT event bus). Additionally: `k1.planner.micro_replan.ready.v1` emitted as TELEMETRY-ONLY for Learning Loop (NOT for delivery).
> - **Testing**: ~30 tests estimated. Trigger detection (~5), micro-SKETCH prompt + output (~6), micro-EXPAND cross-boundary deps (~5), micro-VALIDATE simplified verdict (~5), overlap heuristic (~3), PLAN-12 enforcement (~3), timeout/fallback (~3).
> - **Anti-hallucination**: Micro-replan is an ABBREVIATED pipeline, NOT a full re-plan. It replaces remaining steps ONLY (PLAN-12). It has NO HIL interaction (§10.5.4). It has NO revise loop (§10.6.2). Failure at any stage -> return None (not PLAN FAILED event). The 10s outer timeout is enforced by the CALLER (Orchestrator's asyncio.wait_for), not by PipelineController. Micro-replan output is delivered synchronously (return value), not via event bus. The telemetry event `k1.planner.micro_replan.ready.v1` is consumed by Learning Loop ONLY -- Orchestrator does NOT subscribe to it.

> - [ ] 3 dedicated FSM states in plan_fsm.py [F04]: MICRO_SKETCH, MICRO_EXPAND, MICRO_VALIDATE with transitions per SS18.4
> - [ ] All micro-stage failures -> return None to Orchestrator (NOT plan.failed.v1 event); graceful degradation (SS10.6)
> - [ ] Discovery overlap heuristic revalidation: `_validate_overlap(request)` in PipelineController -- advisory, not blocking (SS10.2.2)
> - [ ] PLAN-12 enforcement: `_enforce_plan12(replacement_plan, completed_ids)` -- no replacement step.id overlaps completed; deps to completed steps valid; no undo/redo of completed (SS10.5.1)
> - [ ] 10s total timeout enforced by CALLER (Orchestrator asyncio.wait_for), NOT by PipelineController; per-stage timeout via StageContext.timeout_remaining_ms (progressive squeeze) (SS10.5.3)
> - [ ] Output: Optional[CommittedPlan] with ONLY replacement steps; CommittedPlan.plan_id is fresh uuid4 (distinct from original_plan_id)
> - [ ] Delivery: synchronous return value (NOT event bus); k1.planner.micro_replan.ready.v1 emitted as TELEMETRY-ONLY for Learning Loop
> - [ ] Cancel between micro-stages via ctx.cancel_check(); on cancel -> CANCELLED -> return None
> - [ ] MicroReplanRequest validation: original_plan_id non-empty, remaining_steps non-empty, trace_id non-empty
> - [ ] `mypy --strict` passes on all modified files (pipeline_controller.py, plan_fsm.py, sketch_service.py, expand_service.py, validate_service.py)
> - [ ] `ruff check` passes on all modified files
> - [ ] ~30 tests: trigger detection (~5), micro-SKETCH (~6), micro-EXPAND cross-boundary (~5), micro-VALIDATE simplified (~5), overlap heuristic (~3), PLAN-12 enforcement (~3), timeout/fallback (~3)

## Milestone 6: Adapters & Factory

> **Goal**: Build all 14 adapters (7 test + 7 production), the PlannerFactory, and kernel bootstrap wiring.

### Epic 6.1: Test Adapters

> **Goal**: Build 7 in-memory test adapters implementing the 7 port Protocols (SS15), plus a shared conftest.py that wires them via `PlannerFactory.create_for_testing()`. Test adapters live in `tests/k1/planner/adapters/` so they are never importable from production code (SS30.3). Every adapter follows the same pattern: constructor injection of preset data, capture-mode recording of all calls, zero I/O, Protocol-structural compliance (SS16.2).
>
> **Reference**: planner.md SS16.2 (test adapter specs), SS16.3 (wiring table), SS30.3 (test adapter file locations), SS30.4 (test file structure), SS15.2-15.8 (port Protocols), SS15.10 (port-to-service dependency matrix).

> **Wiring**: All 7 test adapters in `tests/k1/planner/adapters/` (never in production tree). Each implements the corresponding `typing.Protocol` from `k1/planner/ports/` (F11-F18). conftest.py depends on `PlannerFactory.create_for_testing()` (6.3.3). Test adapters are used by ALL test files in M7. Design principle: test adapters capture calls for assertion, return deterministic preset data, perform zero I/O.
>
> **Anti-hallucination**: TestLLMAdapter stage_responses MUST contain JSON conforming to each stage's OUTPUT_SCHEMA (SS6.3.1, SS7.3.1, SS8.3.1). TestMailboxAdapter depth limit is 5 (not configurable -- matches production MailboxAdapter). TestBridgeAdapter.persist_plan is fire-and-forget (no return value, no exception). TestDeltaAdapter.emit is synchronous (not async).
>
> **Dependencies**: Ports (M1 Epic 1.4), types (M1 Epic 1.2), PlannerFactory.create_for_testing (6.3.3). **Estimated tests**: ~15 (adapter Protocol compliance + fixture wiring). **Files**: 9 (7 adapter files + conftest.py + adapters/\_\_init\_\_.py).

### Epic 6.2: Production Adapters

> **Goal**: Build 7 production adapters (SS16.1) that bridge Planner port Protocols to real K1/K0 infrastructure. Each adapter lives in `k1/planner/adapters/` (F27-F34, SS30.2). Production adapters are wired at kernel boot time by PlannerFactory (6.3). Each wraps exactly one Fabric/K0 port -- no multiplexing, no dynamic switching (SS16.3). V1 note: LLMGatewayAdapter is the V2 production path; V1 uses TestLLMAdapter in all environments (SS16.1.2).
>
> **Reference**: planner.md SS16.1 (production adapter specs), SS16.3 (wiring table), SS15.2-15.8 (port Protocols each adapter implements), SS30.5.5 (file specs F27-F34), SS15.9 (port name mapping Planner vs Fabric).

| 6.2.1 | MailboxAdapter (K1 bus integration) | TODO | k1/planner/adapters/mailbox_adapter.py [F28] | **MailboxAdapter** (SS16.1.1): implements `IMailboxPort` (SS15.2). Constructor: `MailboxAdapter(max_depth: int = 5, priority_class: str = "INTERACTIVE")`. Internal state: `_queue: asyncio.Queue[PlanRequest]` bounded at `max_depth`, `_cancel_set: Set[str]` for pending cancellations, `_plan_lock: asyncio.Lock` for V1 single-plan guard. Methods: `enqueue(request)` -- `_queue.put_nowait()`, raises `MailboxFullError` if full (depth >= max_depth). `send_cancel(request_id)` -- adds to `_cancel_set`; PlannerAgent checks set between stages. `micro_replan(request)` -- acquires `_plan_lock` (waits for current plan to complete if held), delegates to PipelineController.micro_replan(), returns `CommittedPlan` directly (bypasses queue -- SS10.1.1). `dequeue()` -- `await _queue.get()` (blocks until available). Thread safety: `enqueue()` safe for concurrent callers (`asyncio.Queue` is task-safe); `dequeue()` called from PlannerAgent's single mailbox loop; `micro_replan()` serialized by `_plan_lock`. Shutdown: rejects with `ShutdownError` after shutdown signal. MPSC + FIFO semantics, single priority class INTERACTIVE -- no WFQ weighting (SS4.1). |

Constructor: `LLMGatewayAdapter(llm_request_bus: ILLMRequestBus, consumer_id: str = "planner")`. Method: `execute(hub_request) -> HubResponse` -- stamps `hub_request.constraints.consumer_id = "planner"` for cost attribution (MH-11), sends via `_bus.request(hub_request)`, awaits `HubResponse`. Routing path (SS15.3): Service -> ILLMPort.execute -> LLMGatewayAdapter -> LLM_REQUEST_BUS (async request-reply) -> Model Hub RequestRouter -> ModelSelector -> ProviderDispatcher -> Provider Plugin -> HubResponse. Does NOT select models -- Model Hub's ModelSelector chooses (SS13.4). Inherits per-provider circuit breaker protection (MH-05). Error handling: Model Hub timeout -> `LLMTimeoutError` after `constraints.timeout_ms`. Budget exceeded (MH-04) -> `BudgetExceededError`. Provider CB OPEN -> Model Hub handles fallback internally (MH-06). Network/bus failure -> `AdapterException(DEGRADED)`. V1 NOTE: V1 runs TestLLMAdapter in all environments. LLMGatewayAdapter is the V2 production path when Model Hub is live. |

Methods: `discover_capabilities(domain, intent, safety_band, session_context, top_k)` -- `asyncio.wait_for(_fabric.discover_capabilities(...), timeout=_timeout_ms/1000)`. `find_relevant_prompts(intent, domain, safety_band, top_k)` -- same pattern with `_fabric.find_relevant_prompts()`. In-process method call (NOT HTTP, NOT event-based) -- same Python runtime as Fabric, direct object reference (SS16.1.3). Returns `RetrievalResult` (Top-K scored capabilities via 4-stage pipeline: Embed -> HardFilter -> SoftRank -> TopK). Timeout: 50ms per call, retry once on failure. No circuit breaker (in-process call, failure means Fabric itself is broken). Error handling: timeout -> return empty `RetrievalResult` (degraded, not fatal). Exception -> log and return empty result. PLAN-06 enforcement (SS15.4): no `invoke_capability()` or `execute()` method exists on this interface -- execution path is structurally impossible. |

Constructor: `SessionStateReadAdapter(reader: ISessionStateReader, session_id: str)`. Session_id is bound at construction (one adapter instance per session). Methods: `read_sections(sections, trace_id)` -- `_reader.read_sections(_session_id, sections)`. `get_snapshot(trace_id)` -- `_reader.get_snapshot(_session_id)`. Wraps `ISessionStateReader` (Fabric port 5.1.1) with pre-bound `session_id`. Multi-reader lock-free (`ISessionStateReader` guarantees thread safety). Reads HOT CORE + WARM sections (FlatBuffer-backed, <100us serialization). No write capability (PLAN-01 enforced at type level -- no write method exists on interface or implementation). Error handling: SessionState unavailable -> return empty dict (ToolCallRouter degrades gracefully). Individual section missing -> omitted from result dict (not None-valued). Constructor: `BridgeAdapter(bridge_port: IBridgePort)` -- wraps the Fabric's `IBridgePort` (port 5.1.3, K0 Bridge client). Methods: `recall(query, selectors, trace_id)` -- calls `_bridge.query("memory.recall", {query, selectors})`, extracts `.data` from `BridgeCommandResult`. Latency: <100ms (K0 in-process bridge, FAISS index lookup). `persist_plan(committed_plan)` -- calls `_bridge.send_command("memory.store", committed_plan.to_dict())`. Fire-and-forget: CommitService does NOT wait for confirmation (SS9.3.1). WAL write is best-effort; if K0 offline, plan still delivered via IEventPort (plan.ready event is primary delivery). `is_available()` -- `_bridge.is_available()`. Offline handling (SS15.6): when `is_available() == False`, `recall()` returns empty result (no long-term memory context), `persist_plan()` silently drops (plan still delivered via event bus), planning quality degrades but does NOT fail. Error handling: exception -> catch, log, return empty result (planning degrades, never fails). |
| 6.2.6 | DeltaEmitAdapter (Delta Bus integration) | TODO | k1/planner/adapters/delta_bus_adapter.py [F33] | **DeltaBusAdapter** (SS16.1.6): implements `IDeltaEmitPort` (SS15.7). Constructor: `DeltaBusAdapter(delta_bus: IDeltaBusPort, agent_id: str = "planner")`. Method: `emit(delta: DeltaPayload) -> None` -- calls `_bus.emit_delta(_agent_id, delta.delta_type, delta.section, delta.data)` with pre-bound `agent_id`. Publishes to topic `k1.planner.delta.v1`. Fire-and-forget contract: MUST NOT block, MUST NOT raise. Thread-safe (`IDeltaBusPort` guarantees concurrent call safety). Error handling: bus unavailable -> log warning, drop delta (observability loss, not functional failure). Exception -> catch and log (never propagate). Delta loss is acceptable -- deltas are observability signals, not control-plane messages. PLAN-01 emphasis (SS15.7): delta emission is NOT a SessionState write. Deltas go to the Delta Bus, a separate pub/sub channel. SessionState consumers may subscribe and incorporate them, but the Planner has no knowledge of or dependency on this. Emission points (SS15.7): stage_transition (start/complete), tool_result, plan_update (ready/failed). |
| 6.2.7 | EventAdapter (K1 Event Bus integration) | TODO | k1/planner/adapters/event_bus_adapter.py [F34] | **EventBusAdapter** (SS16.1.7): implements `IEventPort` (SS15.8). Constructor: `EventBusAdapter(event_port: IEventPort)` -- wraps Fabric's `IEventPort` (port 5.1.2). Methods: `publish(topic, payload)` -- `_event_port.emit(topic, payload)`. Fire-and-forget for lifecycle events. `subscribe(topic, handler)` -- `_event_port.subscribe(topic, handler)` returning `SubscriptionHandle`. `unsubscribe(handle)` -- `_event_port.unsubscribe(handle)`. Published topics (SS15.8): `k1.planner.plan.ready.v1` (CommittedPlan), `plan.failed.v1` (error details), `plan.cancelled.v1` (cancel reason), `k1.hil.clarification.v1` (question), `k1.hil.approval_request.v1` (summary + options). Subscribed topics: `k1.hil.clarification_response.v1` (HILCoordinator), `k1.hil.approval_response.v1` (HILCoordinator). FAB-09 compliance: all payloads MUST carry `cognitive_trace_id`. Error handling: emit failure -> log and continue (fire-and-forget). Subscription failure -> re-subscribe with backoff (HIL responses are critical). Handler exception -> IEventPort catches and logs (does not propagate). Subscription lifecycle (SS15.8): INIT phase subscribes 2 HIL response topics + 2 plan request/cancel topics; SHUTDOWN unsubscribes all 4. |

> **Done When (Category I — Production Adapters):**
>
> - [ ] 7 production adapter files exist at `k1/planner/adapters/{name}_adapter.py` matching F28-F34
> - [ ] Each adapter implements the corresponding Port Protocol (`isinstance` check passes)
> - [ ] Each constructor wraps exactly ONE Fabric/K0 infrastructure port (no multiplexing, SS16.3)
> - [ ] Error handling returns empty/degraded result on failure (never crashes pipeline)
> - [ ] Fire-and-forget ports (`DeltaBusAdapter.emit`, `BridgeAdapter.persist_plan`): catch all exceptions, log, return silently
> - [ ] `MailboxAdapter` uses `asyncio.Queue` (not `collections.deque`), bounded at `max_depth`
> - [ ] `LLMGatewayAdapter` is V2 only — V1 uses `TestLLMAdapter` in ALL environments (SS16.1.2)
> - [ ] `FabricRetrievalAdapter` is in-process (NOT HTTP, NOT event-based — direct object reference)
> - [ ] `SessionStateReadAdapter` binds `session_id` at construction (not per-call)
> - [ ] `BridgeAdapter.persist_plan` is fire-and-forget (CommitService does NOT await confirmation)
> - [ ] `EventBusAdapter` subscription lifecycle: subscribed at INIT, unsubscribed at SHUTDOWN
> - [ ] All adapters re-exported in `k1/planner/adapters/__init__.py` [F27]
> - [ ] Imports follow Layer 2 rules: Layer 0 + Layer 1 only
> - [ ] `mypy --strict` passes
> - [ ] `ruff check` passes
> - [ ] Tests exist: happy path + error path + degraded path (3 minimum per adapter)

> **Wiring**: 7 production adapters in `k1/planner/adapters/` (F28-F34), plus `adapters/__init__.py` (F27) re-exporting all 7. Each wraps exactly one Fabric/K0 infrastructure port (SS16.3 wiring table). Port-to-adapter mapping: IMailboxPort -> MailboxAdapter -> asyncio.Queue, ILLMPort -> LLMGatewayAdapter -> LLM_REQUEST_BUS, IFabricRetrievalPort -> FabricRetrievalAdapter -> FabricRetrieval API, IStateReadPort -> SessionStateReadAdapter -> ISessionStateReader, IBridgePort -> BridgeAdapter -> K0 Bridge, IDeltaEmitPort -> DeltaBusAdapter -> IDeltaBusPort, IEventPort -> EventBusAdapter -> K1 Event Bus.
>
> **Anti-hallucination**: MailboxAdapter uses asyncio.Queue (not collections.deque). LLMGatewayAdapter is V2 only -- V1 uses TestLLMAdapter. FabricRetrievalAdapter is in-process (not HTTP). SessionStateReadAdapter binds session_id at construction (not per-call). BridgeAdapter.persist_plan is fire-and-forget (CommitService does NOT await confirmation). DeltaBusAdapter.emit is synchronous and never raises. EventBusAdapter subscription lifecycle is managed by PlannerAgent INIT/SHUTDOWN (SS23.1, SS23.5).
>
> **Dependencies**: Ports (M1 Epic 1.4), types (M1 Epic 1.2), Fabric ports (external: ISessionStateReader, IBridgePort, IDeltaBusPort, IEventPort, FabricRetrieval), Model Hub (external: ILLMRequestBus). **Estimated tests**: ~21 (3 per adapter: happy path + error path + degraded path). **Files**: 8 (7 adapter files + adapters/\_\_init\_\_.py).

(2) `hil_coord = HILCoordinator(llm_port, event_port)` -- leaf service, ports: LLM + Event. (3) `sketch = SketchService(llm_port, tool_router, hil_coord)` -- stage service, deps: LLM + ToolCallRouter + HILCoordinator. (4) `expand = ExpandService(llm_port, tool_router)` -- stage service, deps: LLM + ToolCallRouter. (5) `validate = ValidateService(llm_port, fabric_port, hil_coord)` -- stage service, deps: LLM + FabricRetrieval + HILCoordinator. (6) `commit = CommitService(bridge_port, delta_port, event_port)` -- stage service, deps: Bridge + DeltaEmit + Event. No ILLMPort (PLAN-03). (7) `pipeline = PipelineController(sketch, expand, validate, commit, delta_port, event_port)` -- orchestrator, deps: all 4 stages + DeltaEmit + Event. (8) `agent = PlannerAgent(pipeline, mailbox_port, event_port, config)` -- top-level, deps: PipelineController + Mailbox + Event + Config. (9) `await agent.start()` -- runs INIT phase (SS23.1): subscribes events, sets FSM IDLE. (10) Return `agent`. Invariant: step ordering matches SS30.6 dependency layers exactly. No step references an object not yet created. CommitService (step 6) provably has no ILLMPort (PLAN-03 enforced at construction). |

Validation runs at the START of `_wire()`, before any object construction. Checks: (1) **Protocol compliance**: each port argument is checked via `isinstance(port, PortProtocol)` using `@runtime_checkable` Protocol classes (SS15). Raises `InvalidPortError(port_name, expected_protocol, actual_type)` on failure. (2) **Completeness**: all 7 ports must be non-None. Raises `MissingPortError(port_name)` if any is None. (3) **No circular references**: verify that no port object is the same instance used for two different port slots (e.g., passing the same adapter for both LLM and Event would be structurally invalid). Raises `DuplicatePortError` on detection. (4) **Config validation**: verify `PlannerConfig` field values are within bounds (e.g., `mailbox_max_depth >= 1`, `pipeline_timeout_ms > 0`, `max_tool_calls_per_plan >= 1`). Raises `InvalidConfigError` on out-of-bounds. Post-wiring validation: after step 9 (agent.start()), verify `agent.ready() == True` and `agent.health().status == "HEALTHY"`. If not ready after start: raise `PlannerInitError`. All validation errors are structured (error code + message + context dict) for observability. |

These are the Planner's public kernel-integration surface. |

## Milestone 7: Testing

> **Goal**: Implement ~335+ per-service tests, plus integration, contract, invariant, lifecycle, and performance tests. ALL tests follow the no-mock principle: every test is wired through `PlannerFactory.create_for_testing()` with real test adapters, accesses services through the public API only, and verifies events/deltas via capture-mode adapters.

### No-Mock Testing Philosophy

All Planner tests follow six principles derived from the Fabric testing strategy:

| # | Principle | Planner Application |
|---|-----------|---------------------|
| 1 | Real Adapters | 7 test adapters (M6 Epic 6.1) are real implementations with capture mode -- NOT `unittest.mock.Mock`, `MagicMock`, or `patch`. TestLLMAdapter returns deterministic HubResponses. TestEventAdapter captures published events. TestDeltaAdapter captures emitted deltas. |
| 2 | Real Contract Parsing | Tests load and validate real YAML contracts (`wiring.contract.yaml`, `module.contract.yaml`, `pipeline.contract.yaml`) -- not hardcoded expectations. |
| 3 | Real Registry | PlannerFactory.create\_for\_testing() wires the full 10-step object graph (6.3.5) -- same construction as production. No partial wiring. |
| 4 | Real Timers | Performance tests use actual time measurement with short but real timeouts. Circuit breaker tests (external, not Planner-owned) use real timer semantics. |
| 5 | Real Concurrency | Asyncio-based tests use real `asyncio.Lock`, real `asyncio.Queue`, real cooperative cancellation. No simulated concurrency. |
| 6 | Standalone First | All tests work with `PlannerFactory.create_for_testing()` only -- no external services, no network, no database. |

**Forbidden patterns**: `unittest.mock.Mock`, `unittest.mock.MagicMock`, `unittest.mock.patch`, `unittest.mock.AsyncMock`. Any test importing from `unittest.mock` is rejected.

**Why No Mocks?**

| Mock Pattern | Real Alternative | Benefit |
|-------------|-----------------|---------|
| `Mock(spec=ILLMPort)` | `TestLLMAdapter(stage_responses={...})` | Captures all calls, returns valid HubResponses, enforces Protocol compliance |
| `patch("k1.planner.stages.sketch_service.ILLMPort")` | Inject `TestLLMAdapter` via factory | Tests real wiring path, not import-patched shortcuts |
| `MagicMock(return_value=SketchResult(...))` | Configure `TestLLMAdapter` with JSON matching OUTPUT\_SCHEMA | Validates full LLM response parsing path |
| `AsyncMock(side_effect=LLMTimeoutError)` | `TestLLMAdapter(error_stages={"SKETCH"})` | Tests real error propagation through real adapter |

### Epic 7.1: Unit Tests -- PipelineController

**Goal**: ~80 tests covering PipelineController's stage orchestration, budget injection, cancel protocol, and FSM transitions. All tests wired via `PlannerFactory.create_for_testing()`. Tests verify behavior through the factory-wired PipelineController, not through direct method calls on isolated objects.

> **Reference**: planner.md SS17.2 (PipelineController spec, ~80 tests), SS5.2 (26-step pipeline flow), SS13.3 (budget injection), SS18 (Plan FSM), SS23.3 (STAGE\_TRANSITION), SS24.2 (cancel protocol).
>
> - **Test access pattern**: `(agent, adapters) = await PlannerFactory.create_for_testing()` -> enqueue PlanRequest via `adapters["mailbox"].enqueue()` -> await pipeline completion -> assert on `adapters["event"]` and `adapters["delta"]` captures.
> - **HARDCORE TEST REQUIREMENTS** (from Fabric): (1) ALWAYS use PlannerFactory, (2) all invocations through public API (PlannerAgent mailbox), (3) real adapters only (test adapters are real, NOT mocks), (4) verify events via TestEventAdapter capture, (5) verify all invariants hold during execution.

(2) Plan lock released after cancel (next plan can proceed). (3) FSM transitions correctly: current\_state -> CANCELLED -> IDLE. Verify each transition via delta capture `stage_transition{stage, status}`. (2) Micro-replan: IDLE -> MICRO\_SKETCH -> MICRO\_EXPAND -> MICRO\_VALIDATE -> COMMITTING -> COMPLETED -> IDLE. (3) Plan failure at SKETCH: IDLE -> SKETCHING -> FAILED -> IDLE. (4) Plan failure at VALIDATE (reject): IDLE -> ... -> VALIDATING -> FAILED -> IDLE. (5) Cancel between stages: IDLE -> SKETCHING -> CANCELLED -> IDLE. (6) Revise loop: IDLE -> SKETCHING -> EXPANDING -> VALIDATING -> EXPANDING (revise) -> VALIDATING -> COMMITTING -> COMPLETED -> IDLE. **Illegal transition tests** (~8): (1) IDLE -> EXPANDING (skip SKETCH): verify IllegalStateTransitionError or equivalent rejection. (2) SKETCHING -> COMMITTING (skip EXPAND+VALIDATE). (3) COMPLETED -> SKETCHING (terminal state cannot re-enter pipeline). (4) FAILED -> EXPANDING. (5) CANCELLED -> COMMITTING. (6) MICRO\_SKETCH -> EXPANDING (micro to full crossover). (7) SKETCHING -> MICRO\_EXPAND (full to micro crossover). (8) Any state -> self-transition (SKETCHING -> SKETCHING is invalid). **Terminal state tests** (~6): (1) COMPLETED is terminal: emits plan.ready.v1, releases lock, transitions to IDLE. (2) FAILED is terminal: emits plan.failed.v1, releases lock, transitions to IDLE. (3) CANCELLED is terminal: emits plan.cancelled.v1, releases lock, transitions to IDLE. (4) After FAILED -> IDLE: next PlanRequest processes normally (agent not stuck). (5) After CANCELLED -> IDLE: next PlanRequest processes normally. (6) Multiple consecutive plans: IDLE -> ... -> COMPLETED -> IDLE -> ... -> COMPLETED -> IDLE (verify state machine resets correctly). **Guard condition tests** (~5): (1) IDLE -> SKETCHING requires plan lock acquired: verify via asyncio.Lock state. (2) Stage transition requires no cancel pending: verify cancel\_check() is called between stages. (3) VALIDATING -> COMMITTING requires ValidationVerdict.status == "approved" (or "revise" treated as approved in micro-replan). (4) COMMITTING -> COMPLETED requires CommittedPlan assembled + event published. (5) Verify `cancel_check()` callable is bound to correct request\_id (not stale from previous plan). |

Cancel tests use real cooperative cancellation (flag-based, checked at real checkpoints), not simulated preemption.

### Epic 7.2: Unit Tests -- Stage Services

**Goal**: ~185 tests across 4 stage services (SketchService ~60, ExpandService ~50, ValidateService ~45, CommitService ~30). All tests wired via factory. Each test configures adapter behavior (LLM responses, tool results, state context) BEFORE factory wiring, then verifies stage behavior through the pipeline.

> **Reference**: planner.md SS17.3 (SketchService), SS17.4 (ExpandService), SS17.5 (ValidateService), SS17.6 (CommitService), SS6 (SKETCH stage), SS7 (EXPAND stage), SS8 (VALIDATE stage), SS9 (COMMIT stage).
>
> - **Test pattern**: Configure TestLLMAdapter with stage-specific responses -> wire via factory -> enqueue PlanRequest -> verify stage output through event/delta captures and LLM call log.
> - **Stage isolation through adapter configuration**: To test SKETCH in isolation, configure TestLLMAdapter to return valid responses for SKETCH but error for EXPAND -> verify SKETCH output through delta capture before pipeline fails at EXPAND. Alternatively, configure all stages for success and inspect per-stage behavior via `_call_log` and delta captures.

### Epic 7.4: Integration Tests

**Goal**: 5 end-to-end integration tests exercising the full factory-wired Planner through complete scenarios. Each test uses `PlannerFactory.create_for_testing()`, enqueues a real PlanRequest through the mailbox, and verifies the complete output chain: events, deltas, adapter captures, and plan structure.

> **Reference**: planner.md SS5.2 (26-step pipeline flow), SS10 (micro-replan), SS12 (HIL), SS19 (error recovery), SS24.2 (cancel protocol).
>
> **HARDCORE INTEGRATION TEST REQUIREMENTS** (from Fabric testing strategy):
>
> 1. ALWAYS use PlannerFactory.create\_for\_testing()
> 2. All invocations through public API -- enqueue via mailbox, NEVER call PipelineController directly
> 3. Real adapters only (7 test adapters from M6 6.1 are real implementations, NOT mocks)
> 4. Verify events via TestEventAdapter capture mode
> 5. Verify deltas via TestDeltaAdapter capture mode
> 6. Verify ALL invariants hold during execution (PLAN-01 through PLAN-12)
> 7. Verify trace\_id propagation end-to-end (FAB-09)

### Epic 7.5: Contract Tests

**Goal**: Verify Planner module compliance with its declared contracts (port Protocols, event schemas, wiring contract). Contract tests load real YAML contracts and validate code against them.

> **Reference**: planner.md SS30.1 (contract files), SS30.2 (39 required files), SS15 (port Protocol specs), SS28 (event topic catalog), wiring.contract.yaml (593 lines), module.contract.yaml (173 lines), pipeline.contract.yaml.
>
> - **Contract test principle**: Parse REAL contract YAML files, compare against REAL code artifacts. No hardcoded expectations that could drift from contracts.

(2) **Method signature compliance**: verify each adapter implements ALL methods declared in the Protocol with correct parameter names and return types. ### Epic 7.6: Invariant Tests

**Goal**: Explicit enforcement tests for all 12 hard invariants (PLAN-01 through PLAN-12). Each invariant is tested by attempting to violate it through the factory-wired pipeline and verifying the violation is prevented.

> **Reference**: planner.md SS2 (invariant table), SS15 (port-level enforcement), SS17 (service-level enforcement), SS30.7 (port-to-service matrix).
>
> - **Test approach**: attempt invariant violation through valid API paths (adapter configuration, PlanRequest construction) and verify the invariant holds. Do NOT test artificial scenarios that cannot occur through the pipeline.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 7.6.1 | PLAN-01 through PLAN-06 enforcement tests | TODO | tests/k1/planner/invariants/test\_invariants\_01\_06.py | **PLAN-01 (Never writes SessionState)** (~3): (1) Run full pipeline -> verify TestStateReadAdapter has ZERO write calls (only `_read_log` populated, no `_write_log` exists). (2) Verify IStateReadPort Protocol has no write methods via `inspect.getmembers()`. (3) Verify no adapter in the factory-wired graph writes to SessionState (scan all adapter captures). **PLAN-02 (Read-only tools only)** (~3): (1) Verify ToolCallRouter routing table contains only read-only tools (discover\_capabilities, find\_relevant\_prompts, query\_planning\_context, recall\_for\_planning). (2) Run full pipeline -> verify NO tool call modifies external state (all adapter captures are read operations). (3) Verify IFabricRetrievalPort has no execute/write/invoke methods. **PLAN-03 (COMMIT is deterministic, zero LLM)** (~3): (1) Run full pipeline -> verify TestLLMAdapter.\_call\_log has zero calls with COMMIT stage identifier. (2) Verify CommitService constructor signature has no ILLMPort parameter. (3) Run COMMIT twice with identical inputs -> verify identical outputs (excluding UUID plan\_id and created\_at timestamp). **PLAN-04 (45s max pipeline timeout)** (~3): (1) Configure TestLLMAdapter with high latency (50s) -> verify pipeline terminates with plan.failed.v1{reason: "timeout"} before 50s completes. (2) Verify PlannerConfig.pipeline\_timeout\_ms default == 45000. (3) Verify StageContext.timeout\_remaining\_ms starts at 45000 and decreases across stages. **PLAN-05 (Max 6 tool calls per plan)** (~3): (1) Configure TestLLMAdapter to request many tool calls -> verify ToolCallRouter stops at 6 (BudgetExhaustedError or rejection). (2) Run full pipeline with standard tool usage -> verify total tool calls <= 6 via all adapter captures combined. (3) Verify counter resets between plans. **PLAN-06 (Never executes capabilities)** (~2): (1) Verify IFabricRetrievalPort has NO invoke\_capability/execute\_capability methods. (2) Run full pipeline -> verify TestFabricRetrievalAdapter received ONLY discover/find\_relevant calls, zero execute calls. |
| 7.6.2 | PLAN-07 through PLAN-12 enforcement tests | TODO | tests/k1/planner/invariants/test\_invariants\_07\_12.py | **PLAN-07 (Token budget -- V2 deferred)** (~1): Verify PlanStep.token\_budget field does not exist or is None/0 in all issued steps (V1 deferred). **PLAN-08 (All capabilities must exist in Fabric)** (~3): (1) Configure TestFabricRetrievalAdapter with 3 capabilities -> configure TestLLMAdapter EXPAND response to reference a capability NOT in the 3 -> ValidateService deterministic check catches `capability_missing` -> ValidationVerdict includes issue. (2) All capabilities exist -> check passes. (3) Verify via `adapters["fabric"]._discover_log` that capability existence check was performed. **PLAN-09 (Plan must be DAG, no cycles)** (~3): (1) Configure TestLLMAdapter EXPAND response with cyclic dependencies (s1->s2->s1) -> ValidateService catches `dag_cycle` -> plan.failed.v1 or revise. (2) Valid DAG -> passes. (3) Complex DAG with diamond pattern -> passes (not a cycle). **PLAN-10 (HIL max 2 rounds)** (~3): (1) Configure HIL to trigger 2 rounds (1 clarification + 1 approval) -> both complete. (2) Attempt to trigger 3rd round -> budget exceeded, best-effort proceed. (3) Verify round counter in delta/log. **PLAN-11 (All LLM calls carry budget)** (~3): (1) Run full pipeline -> verify EVERY entry in TestLLMAdapter.\_call\_log has non-null constraints (max\_tokens > 0, timeout\_ms > 0). (2) Verify no LLM call is made without budget constraints. (2) Verify replacement step deps referencing s1/s2 are valid cross-boundary deps (completed IDs). (3) CommittedPlan.steps contains ONLY replacement steps (no completed steps included). |

> **Wiring**: Tests in `tests/k1/planner/invariants/` directory. Each test runs the full factory-wired pipeline and verifies invariant enforcement through adapter captures and output inspection. Invariant violations are triggered through adapter configuration (TestLLMAdapter responses that would violate if not caught).
>
> **Anti-hallucination**: Invariant tests do NOT bypass the pipeline to test invariants. They attempt violations through the REAL pipeline path and verify the pipeline prevents them. PLAN-03 is enforced at constructor level (CommitService has no ILLMPort param), not at runtime check level. PLAN-01 is enforced at Protocol level (IStateReadPort has no write methods), not at adapter level.
>
> **Dependencies**: Epic 7.0 (infrastructure), M1 (invariant definitions), M6 (factory). **Estimated tests**: ~30 across 2 issues. **Files**: 2 test files in invariants/ directory.

### Epic 7.7: Lifecycle Tests

**Goal**: Test the 3 exceptional lifecycle phases (INIT, SHUTDOWN, CRASH\_RECOVERY) through the factory-wired PlannerAgent. Verify service creation, event subscription, graceful shutdown, and V1 crash recovery behavior.

> **Reference**: planner.md SS23.1 (INIT), SS23.5 (SHUTDOWN), SS23.6 (CRASH\_RECOVERY), SS29.1 (kernel Phase 5), SS29.3 (shutdown ordering).

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 7.7.1 | INIT phase tests | TODO | tests/k1/planner/lifecycle/test\_init.py | **INIT lifecycle tests** (~8). (1) **Service creation**: after `PlannerFactory.create_for_testing()`, verify PlannerAgent has all 7 internal services created (PipelineController, SketchService, ExpandService, ValidateService, CommitService, ToolCallRouter, HILCoordinator). Access via `agent._pipeline` (PipelineController holds refs to stages). (2) **Port wiring**: verify all 7 ports are wired (agent can accept PlanRequest and process through pipeline). Run a simple plan to verify wiring is complete. (3) **Event subscriptions**: verify 4 event subscriptions registered via TestEventAdapter.\_subscriptions: plan.request.v1, plan.cancel.v1, clarification\_response.v1, approval\_response.v1. (4) **FSM initial state**: verify Plan FSM is in IDLE state after INIT. (5) **Plan lock released**: verify `_plan_lock` is not held after INIT (ready to accept plans). (6) **Mailbox empty**: verify mailbox queue is empty after INIT. (7) **Agent health check**: verify `agent.ready() == True` and `agent.health().status == "HEALTHY"` after factory creation. (8) **Config propagation**: verify PlannerConfig values are accessible by services (e.g., pipeline\_timeout\_ms, max\_tool\_calls, max\_hil\_rounds match config). |
| 7.7.2 | SHUTDOWN phase tests | TODO | tests/k1/planner/lifecycle/test\_shutdown.py | **SHUTDOWN lifecycle tests** (~10). **Clean shutdown** (~4): (1) No in-flight plan: call `agent.shutdown()` -> verify mailbox rejects new enqueues (ShutdownError), event subscriptions unsubscribed, structured log `shutdown.complete` emitted. (2) Empty mailbox drain: no queued requests -> drain is no-op. (3) Verify shutdown completes within grace period (5s default). (4) After shutdown: verify `agent.ready() == False`. **Shutdown with queued requests** (~3): (1) Enqueue 3 PlanRequests, do NOT start processing -> call shutdown -> all 3 cancelled with `plan.cancelled.v1{reason: "shutdown"}`. Verify 3 cancel events via TestEventAdapter. (2) Verify drained request\_ids match enqueued request\_ids. (3) Verify Orchestrator-side PendingPlanContext timeout reaper can detect shutdown cancels. **Shutdown with in-flight plan** (~3): (1) Start a plan (SKETCH in progress) -> call shutdown -> cancel flag set -> PipelineController detects at next checkpoint -> `plan.cancelled.v1{reason: "shutdown"}` -> pipeline stops -> lock released. (2) Verify deltas flushed (best-effort). (3) Verify partial plan state persisted to WAL (fire-and-forget via IBridgePort). Verify `adapters["bridge"]._persist_capture` has partial state entry. |
| 7.7.3 | CRASH\_RECOVERY phase tests | TODO | tests/k1/planner/lifecycle/test\_crash\_recovery.py | **CRASH\_RECOVERY lifecycle tests** (~5). V1 simplification: full recovery is V2 (SS23.6). V1 tests verify the check-and-discard behavior. (1) **No crash state**: TestBridgeAdapter configured with empty recall for "planner\_in\_flight" -> no recovery action -> INIT proceeds to mailbox loop normally. Verify no recovery delta emitted. (2) **Stale crash state**: TestBridgeAdapter configured with partial plan from >120s ago -> Planner discards (too stale to resume). Verify `plan.failed.v1{reason: "crash_recovery_discard"}` emitted. Verify recovery delta: `{action: "discarded", staleness_ms: >120000}`. (3) **V1 always discards**: Even fresh partial plan (< 120s) is discarded in V1 (SS23.6 "V1 simplification"). Verify behavior matches V1 specification. (4) **Recovery clears WAL**: After discard, verify partial plan state is cleared from Bridge. (5) **Post-recovery normal operation**: After crash recovery, PlannerAgent enters normal mailbox loop and processes new PlanRequests successfully. Enqueue a PlanRequest after crash recovery -> verify plan.ready.v1 emitted. |

> **Done When (Category K — Lifecycle Tests):**
>
> - [ ] Test files exist: `test_init.py`, `test_shutdown.py`, `test_crash_recovery.py` in `tests/k1/planner/lifecycle/`
> - [ ] ALL tests use `PlannerFactory.create_for_testing()` (INIT runs internally during factory creation)
> - [ ] No imports from `unittest.mock` (FORBIDDEN)
> - [ ] INIT tests (~8): service creation, port wiring, 4 event subscriptions, FSM IDLE, plan lock released, mailbox empty, `agent.ready() == True`, config propagation
> - [ ] SHUTDOWN tests (~10): clean shutdown (unsubscribe + log), shutdown with queued requests (drain + cancel), shutdown with in-flight plan (cancel flag + flush + persist)
> - [ ] CRASH_RECOVERY tests (~5): no crash state (no-op), stale state (discard), V1 always discards, WAL cleared after discard, post-recovery normal operation
> - [ ] Shutdown grace period verified (5s default from `PlannerConfig.shutdown_grace_period_ms`)
> - [ ] Post-shutdown: `agent.ready() == False`
> - [ ] Post-crash-recovery: PlannerAgent enters normal mailbox loop and processes new PlanRequests
> - [ ] Test count meets or exceeds ~23 total across 3 files
> - [ ] All tests pass: `pytest tests/k1/planner/lifecycle/ -v`

> **Wiring**: Tests in `tests/k1/planner/lifecycle/` directory. Lifecycle tests access PlannerAgent internal state where necessary (FSM state, plan lock status) to verify lifecycle phase behavior. SHUTDOWN tests require timing control (enqueue requests, start processing, trigger shutdown mid-pipeline).
>
> **Anti-hallucination**: INIT tests verify through factory -- `PlannerFactory.create_for_testing()` runs INIT internally. SHUTDOWN tests call `agent.shutdown()` (real method, not simulated). V1 CRASH\_RECOVERY always discards (no resume logic in V1). Lifecycle tests may access internal state (`agent._pipeline`, `agent._plan_lock`) for verification -- this is acceptable in lifecycle tests that verify construction and teardown, not in functional tests.
>
> **Dependencies**: Epic 7.0, M6 (factory, adapters), M2 (PlannerAgent). **Estimated tests**: ~23 across 3 issues. **Files**: 3 test files in lifecycle/ directory.

### Epic 7.8: Performance Tests

**Goal**: Benchmark tests validating Planner performance meets SLI targets (SS21). Tests use real timing, real adapters, and real pipeline execution. Performance tests are marked with `@pytest.mark.benchmark` for selective execution.

> **Reference**: planner.md SS21.1 (Planner-owned latencies), SS21.2 (resource budgets), SS22.3.4 (SLI alignment), SS24.1 (V1 single-plan), SS24.3 (micro-replan concurrency).

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 7.8.1 | Pipeline latency benchmarks (12s P50, 30s P99) | TODO | tests/k1/planner/performance/test\_latency\_benchmarks.py | **Pipeline latency tests** (~8). All tests use `PlannerFactory.create_for_testing()` with TestLLMAdapter configured with realistic latency\_ms values. `@pytest.mark.benchmark` marker for selective CI/CD execution. **Full pipeline latency** (~4): (1) Configure TestLLMAdapter with latency: SKETCH=4000ms, EXPAND=3000ms, VALIDATE=2000ms. Run full pipeline. Measure wall-clock time from PlanRequest enqueue to plan.ready.v1. Assert < 12s (P50 target + overhead). (2) P99 target: configure higher latency (SKETCH=7000ms, EXPAND=4500ms, VALIDATE=2500ms). Assert < 30s. (3) Multiple runs (10x): measure distribution, assert P50 < 12s. (4) Verify per-stage latency via delta timestamps: SKETCH < 8s, EXPAND < 5s, VALIDATE < 3s, COMMIT < 100ms. **Micro-replan latency** (~2): (1) Configure micro-replan with latency: Micro-SKETCH=4000ms, Micro-EXPAND=2500ms, Micro-VALIDATE=1500ms. Assert total < 10s (micro-replan budget). (2) Timeout test: configure latency exceeding 10s -> verify caller-side timeout activates, returns None. **Delivery latency** (~2): (1) Measure time from COMMIT completion to plan.ready.v1 event publication. Assert < 10ms (P99). (2) Measure time from plan.ready.v1 to delta plan\_end emission. Assert < 10ms. |
| 7.8.2 | Token budget compliance tests per stage | TODO | tests/k1/planner/performance/test\_token\_compliance.py | **Token budget compliance tests** (~8). Verify token consumption stays within declared budgets. **Per-stage compliance** (~4): (1) SKETCH: verify `adapters["llm"]._call_log` SKETCH call has `hub_response.metadata.usage.total_tokens <= 2048`. Configure TestLLMAdapter to return realistic token counts (e.g., prompt\_tokens=800, completion\_tokens=600, total=1400). (2) EXPAND: total\_tokens <= 1024. (3) VALIDATE: total\_tokens <= 512. (4) COMMIT: total\_tokens == 0. **Total budget compliance** (~2): (1) Sum all stage tokens from LLM call log -> assert <= 3500 (total budget). (2) Micro-replan total tokens <= 2000. **Budget tracking accuracy** (~2): (1) Verify `plan_end` delta `data["total_tokens"]` matches sum of `hub_response.metadata.usage.total_tokens` across all LLM calls. (2) Verify `plan.completed` structured log total\_tokens matches delta total. |
| 7.8.3 | Concurrency stress tests (single-plan lock) | TODO | tests/k1/planner/performance/test\_concurrency\_stress.py | **Concurrency stress tests** (~8). Verify V1 single-plan lock behavior under concurrent load. `@pytest.mark.benchmark`. **Mailbox queuing** (~3): (1) Enqueue 5 PlanRequests rapidly (mailbox max depth) -> all 5 queued, processed serially in FIFO order. Verify via `plan.ready.v1` event ordering (request\_ids match enqueue order). (2) Enqueue 6th request (overflow) -> MailboxFullError raised, PlanAck(REJECTED). (3) Rapid enqueue/dequeue: enqueue while pipeline running -> queued, processed after current plan completes. **Single-plan serialization** (~3): (1) Start plan A (in EXPANDING stage) -> enqueue plan B -> verify plan B waits until plan A completes. Verify via timestamps: plan B start > plan A end. (2) Start plan A -> call micro\_replan() -> micro\_replan waits for plan A to complete (locks serialized). If 10s timeout expires first, returns None. (3) Multiple sequential plans: enqueue 3 plans -> all 3 complete in order, no state leakage between plans (token counters reset, FSM returns to IDLE between plans, tool counters reset). **Cancel under load** (~2): (1) 3 plans queued -> cancel plan 2 -> plan 1 completes, plan 2 cancelled immediately at dequeue (pre-execution cancel), plan 3 processes normally. Verify 2 `plan.ready.v1` + 1 `plan.cancelled.v1`. (2) Cancel in-flight plan while new requests queued -> in-flight cancelled, next request from queue processes. |

Performance tests use TestLLMAdapter configured with realistic `latency_ms` to simulate LLM response times. `@pytest.mark.benchmark` marker allows selective execution (excluded from normal CI, run in nightly/release pipelines).
>
> **Anti-hallucination**: Performance tests use REAL timing (`time.monotonic()` or `asyncio` event loop time), not simulated delays. Token budgets are verified through TestLLMAdapter response metadata (real HubResponse objects with real token counts), not through mocked return values. Concurrency tests use real `asyncio.Lock` and `asyncio.Queue` behavior.
>
> **Dependencies**: Epic 7.0 (infrastructure), M6 (factory). **Estimated tests**: ~24 across 3 issues. **Files**: 3 test files in performance/ directory.

## Milestone 8: Observability

> **Goal**: Instrument the Planner with metrics, tracing, structured logging, and delta emissions. All observability is additive -- it augments the existing pipeline code (M2-M5) with instrumentation hooks. No observability code alters pipeline control flow. No observability failure blocks the pipeline (fire-and-forget semantics for deltas, best-effort for logs and metrics).

### Epic 8.1: Metrics

**Goal**: Implement the 19 Planner metric objects (9 counters, 6 histograms, 4 gauges) defined in planner.md SS22.3, wired into the K1 telemetry subsystem. Metrics are emitted from PipelineController, stage services, ToolCallRouter, HILCoordinator, and MailboxAdapter at the emission points cataloged in SS22.3.1-22.3.3.

> **Reference**: planner.md SS22.3 (full metric catalog), SS22.3.1 (9 counters with labels), SS22.3.2 (6 histograms with buckets), SS22.3.3 (4 gauges), SS22.3.4 (SLI alignment table), SS21 (performance targets), SS30.5.1 F09 (metrics.py file spec).
>
> - **metrics.py** [F09]: central file defining all metric objects. Services import from this file -- they do NOT create their own metric instances.
> - **Telemetry integration**: metrics are emitted via K1 telemetry subsystem (`k1/telemetry/`). Planner metric names are prefixed with `planner.` to avoid namespace collisions.
> - **Emission pattern**: each service method calls the relevant metric at the defined emission point (e.g., `metrics.stage_duration_ms.observe(duration, labels={"stage": "SKETCH"})` after SKETCH completes). No metric call is blocking or async.

(2) `plan_degraded = Counter("planner.plan.degraded", labels=["stage"])` -- incremented when error recovery fallback activates (ERR\_EXPAND\_FAIL, ERR\_VALIDATE\_FAIL). (3) `stage_total = Counter("planner.stage.total", labels=["stage", "outcome"])` -- incremented at stage completion with `stage={SKETCH,EXPAND,VALIDATE,COMMIT}` x `outcome={success,retry,fallback,failed}`. (4) `llm_calls = Counter("planner.llm.calls", labels=["stage", "outcome"])` -- incremented after each LLM call returns with `stage` and `outcome={success,timeout,error}`. (5) `tool_calls = Counter("planner.tool.calls", labels=["tool", "outcome"])` -- incremented by ToolCallRouter after each discovery tool call with `tool={discover_capabilities,find_relevant_prompts,query_planning_context,recall_for_planning}` x `outcome={success,timeout,empty}`. (6) `hil_rounds = Counter("planner.hil.rounds", labels=["type", "outcome"])` -- incremented by HILCoordinator with `type={clarification,approval}` x `outcome={responded,timeout,auto_approved}`. (7) `micro_replan_total = Counter("planner.micro_replan.total", labels=["outcome"])` -- at micro-replan completion `outcome={success,failed}`. (8) `mailbox_rejected = Counter("planner.mailbox.rejected")` -- incremented by MailboxAdapter on depth > 5 rejection. (9) `recovery_activated = Counter("planner.recovery.activated", labels=["path"])` -- incremented when named error path entered with `path={ERR_SKETCH_FAIL,ERR_EXPAND_FAIL,ERR_VALIDATE_FAIL,ERR_COMMIT_FAIL,ERR_HIL_TIMEOUT}`. **Emission wiring**: PipelineController imports `metrics` module and calls counters at each defined emission point (SS22.3.1 "Incremented When" column). (2) `stage_duration_ms = Histogram("planner.stage.duration_ms", labels=["stage"], buckets=[500, 1000, 2000, 5000, 8000, 15000])` -- observed at each stage completion with per-stage wall-clock ms. (3) `llm_latency_ms = Histogram("planner.llm.latency_ms", labels=["stage"], buckets=[500, 1000, 2000, 5000, 8000])` -- observed after each LLM call return from `HubResponse.metadata.latency_ms` or wall-clock delta. (4) `llm_tokens = Histogram("planner.llm.tokens", labels=["stage"], buckets=[256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096])` -- observed after LLM call from `hub_response.metadata.usage.total_tokens`. PipelineController computes total plan duration from pipeline start to PLAN\_END. **SLI alignment** (SS22.3.4): histogram data feeds SLI dashboards -- SKETCH P50 4s / P99 8s, EXPAND P50 3s / P99 5s, VALIDATE P50 2s / P99 3s, COMMIT P50 20ms / P99 100ms, Total P50 12s / P99 30s. |

(2) `plan_in_flight = Gauge("planner.plan.in_flight")` -- set to 1 by PlannerAgent at `_plan_lock.acquire()`, set to 0 at `_plan_lock.release()`. V1: always 0 or 1. (3) `tokens_remaining = Gauge("planner.tokens.budget_remaining")` -- set after each LLM call to `total_token_budget - sum(stage_token_usage.values())`. Starts at 3500, decreases after SKETCH, EXPAND, VALIDATE. Micro-replan starts at 2000. (4) `tool_budget_remaining = Gauge("planner.tool.budget_remaining")` -- set after each tool call to `max_tool_calls - tool_call_count`. Starts at 6, decreases per tool call (PLAN-05). **Emission wiring**: MailboxAdapter calls `metrics.mailbox_depth.set()` in `enqueue()` and `dequeue()`. PlannerAgent calls `metrics.plan_in_flight.set()` at lock acquire/release. PipelineController calls `metrics.tokens_remaining.set()` and `metrics.tool_budget_remaining.set()` after each LLM/tool call respectively. Gauges reset implicitly: `mailbox_depth` reflects real queue size, `plan_in_flight` reflects real lock state, `tokens_remaining` resets at each plan start (PipelineController.reset()), `tool_budget_remaining` resets at plan start. |

This issue wires the error classification counters from 8.1.1 into the actual error handling code paths. **Recovery path counters**: each of the 6 named error recovery paths (SS19) increments `metrics.recovery_activated.inc(labels={"path": "ERR_SKETCH_FAIL"})` when the path is entered. PipelineController calls this in the `_handle_stage_error()` method at the point where the recovery path is selected. **Degradation counters**: `metrics.plan_degraded.inc(labels={"stage": "EXPAND"})` when EXPAND falls back to SKETCH output (ERR\_EXPAND\_FAIL path). `metrics.plan_degraded.inc(labels={"stage": "VALIDATE"})` when ValidateService auto-approves due to arbiter timeout (ERR\_VALIDATE\_FAIL). **Plan outcome counters**: `metrics.plan_total.inc(labels={"outcome": "ready"})` at PLAN\_END when FSM reaches COMPLETED. `metrics.plan_total.inc(labels={"outcome": "failed"})` at PLAN\_END when FSM reaches FAILED. `metrics.plan_total.inc(labels={"outcome": "cancelled"})` at PLAN\_END when FSM reaches CANCELLED. **Stage outcome counters**: `metrics.stage_total.inc(labels={"stage": "SKETCH", "outcome": "success"})` on normal completion, `"retry"` on retry attempt, `"fallback"` on degraded fallback, `"failed"` on unrecoverable failure. **LLM error counters**: `metrics.llm_calls.inc(labels={"stage": "SKETCH", "outcome": "timeout"})` on LLM timeout, `"error"` on other errors. **Tool error counters**: `metrics.tool_calls.inc(labels={"tool": "discover_capabilities", "outcome": "timeout"})` on tool timeout, `"empty"` when tool returns 0 results. |

> **Wiring**: metrics.py [F09] defines all 19 metric objects as module-level constants. PipelineController, stage services, ToolCallRouter, HILCoordinator, and MailboxAdapter import from metrics.py and call metric methods at defined emission points. No metric call is async or blocking. Metric failure (e.g., telemetry subsystem down) is silently caught -- no pipeline impact.
>
> **Anti-hallucination**: Metric objects are defined ONCE in metrics.py and imported by services -- services do NOT create their own Counter/Histogram/Gauge. The Planner does NOT emit CB-specific metrics (CB\_PLANNER metrics owned by Orchestrator, CB\_LLM metrics owned by Model Hub per SS20.8). No metric call alters pipeline control flow. Buckets match SS22.3.2 exactly.
>
> **Dependencies**: M2 (PipelineController), M3 (stage services), M4 (ToolCallRouter, HILCoordinator), M6 (MailboxAdapter), K1 telemetry subsystem (external). **Estimated files**: 1 new (metrics.py), 8 modified (pipeline\_controller.py, 4 stage services, tool\_call\_router.py, hil\_coordinator.py, mailbox\_adapter.py). **Testing**: metric emission verified in M7 tests via capture (TestMetricAdapter or assertion on metric values).

> - [ ] 6 histogram buckets match SS22.3.2 exactly: `plan_duration_ms`, `stage_duration_ms`, `llm_latency_ms`, `llm_tokens`, `tool_latency_ms`, `micro_replan_ms`

### Epic 8.2: Tracing

**Goal**: Implement trace context propagation (cognitive\_trace\_id / FAB-09) across all port calls, span creation per pipeline stage, and cross-service trace linking. The Planner NEVER generates its own trace ID -- it always echoes the caller's `trace_id` from PlanRequest.

> **Reference**: planner.md SS22.1 (trace propagation), SS22.1.1 (trace origin), SS22.1.2 (trace propagation map -- 8 port calls), SS22.1.3 (trace in output messages -- 7 event types), SS22.1.4 (trace invariant), SS30.5.1 F10 (tracing.py file spec).
>
> - **tracing.py** [F10]: utility module with `stamp_trace_id()`, `create_stage_context()`, `log_event()`. Services import these helpers rather than manually stamping trace\_id.
> - **Trace invariant** (SS22.1.4): for every PlanRequest with trace\_id T, ALL port calls, ALL emitted events, ALL structured logs, and ALL deltas MUST carry trace\_id == T.
> - **The Planner does NOT own tracing infrastructure** -- it uses K1 telemetry's trace propagation. tracing.py provides convenience wrappers.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 8.2.1 | Trace context propagation (plan\_id, stage, trace\_id) | TODO | k1/planner/tracing.py [F10] + k1/planner/pipeline\_controller.py [F03] | **tracing.py helper functions** (SS30.5.1 F10). **`stamp_trace_id(hub_request: HubRequest, trace_id: str) -> HubRequest`**: stamps `trace_id` onto HubRequest before sending to ILLMPort. Called by SketchService, ExpandService, ValidateService before every `_llm_port.execute()` call. Returns new HubRequest with trace\_id set (frozen dataclass, create copy). **`create_stage_context(plan_request: PlanRequest, config: PlannerConfig, cancel_check: Callable[[], bool], timeout_remaining_ms: int) -> StageContext`**: creates StageContext with `trace_id=plan_request.trace_id`, `request_id=plan_request.request_id`, `timeout_remaining_ms`, `token_budget_remaining`, `cancel_check`. Called by PipelineController at start of each stage. Centralizes trace\_id propagation so no service needs to read PlanRequest directly. **`log_event(event_name: str, trace_id: str, **fields) -> None`**: emits structured JSON log record with `trace_id`, `timestamp_ms`, `level`, `component="planner"`, and caller-provided fields. Wraps K1 telemetry logger. Used by all services for structured logging (SS22.2). **Trace propagation wiring in PipelineController**: at pipeline start, `_trace_id = plan_request.trace_id` stored as instance field. Every StageContext created during this pipeline carries this trace\_id. Every delta emitted carries this trace\_id. Every event published carries this trace\_id via payload. **Trace propagation map** (SS22.1.2): 8 outbound port calls propagation verified: ILLMPort (HubRequest.trace\_id), IFabricRetrievalPort (method param trace\_id), IStateReadPort (method param trace\_id), IBridgePort recall/persist (method param / CommittedPlan.trace\_id), IDeltaEmitPort (DeltaPayload.trace\_id), IEventPort (payload.trace\_id). |
| 8.2.2 | Span creation per pipeline stage | TODO | k1/planner/tracing.py [F10] + k1/planner/pipeline\_controller.py [F03] | **Span helpers for per-stage trace boundaries**. Planner uses K1 telemetry's span API to create trace spans around each pipeline stage for distributed tracing visualization. **`start_stage_span(trace_id: str, stage: str, request_id: str) -> SpanContext`**: creates a child span under the current trace with `span_name="planner.{stage}"` (e.g., `planner.SKETCH`, `planner.EXPAND`). Attributes: `request_id`, `stage`, `component="planner"`, `trace_id`. Returns SpanContext for span lifecycle management. **`end_stage_span(span: SpanContext, outcome: str, duration_ms: int, tokens_used: int)`**: ends the span with status (OK/ERROR), tags outcome, duration, token usage. **PipelineController integration**: at stage start: `span = tracing.start_stage_span(trace_id, "SKETCH", request_id)`. At stage end: `tracing.end_stage_span(span, "success", duration_ms, tokens)`. On stage error: `tracing.end_stage_span(span, "error", duration_ms, 0)`. Span hierarchy: `planner.pipeline` (parent) -> `planner.SKETCH` -> `planner.EXPAND` -> `planner.VALIDATE` -> `planner.COMMIT` (children). **Micro-replan spans**: `planner.micro_replan` (parent) -> `planner.MICRO_SKETCH` -> `planner.MICRO_EXPAND` -> `planner.MICRO_VALIDATE` -> `planner.COMMIT`. **LLM call sub-spans**: optionally create `planner.llm_call` sub-spans within each stage span for LLM latency visualization. **Tool call sub-spans**: optionally create `planner.tool_call` sub-spans for discovery tool latency. **Design note**: span creation is best-effort. If K1 telemetry is unavailable, span functions are no-ops. Pipeline continues regardless. |
| 8.2.3 | Cross-service trace linking (Orchestrator -> Planner -> Model Hub) | TODO | k1/planner/tracing.py [F10] | **Cross-service trace correlation**. The `cognitive_trace_id` (aliased as `trace_id`) is the single correlation key connecting: Concierge -> Orchestrator -> Planner -> Fabric/Model Hub/K0 (SS22.1.1). **Upstream link**: Planner receives `trace_id` from Orchestrator inside `PlanRequest.trace_id`. Planner NEVER generates its own trace ID. **Downstream links**: (1) **Model Hub**: `HubRequest.trace_id` is set by `tracing.stamp_trace_id()` before every `ILLMPort.execute()` call. Model Hub uses this for MH-03 tracing and cost attribution (MH-11 consumer\_id="planner"). (2) **Fabric Retrieval**: `discover_capabilities(trace_id=...)` and `find_relevant_prompts(trace_id=...)` carry trace\_id as method parameter. Fabric Retrieval uses FAB-09 for internal tracing. (3) **SessionState**: `read_sections(trace_id=...)` carries trace\_id for read operation correlation. (4) **K0 Bridge**: `recall(trace_id=...)` and `persist_plan(committed_plan)` where `committed_plan.trace_id` carries the correlation key. (5) **Delta Bus**: every DeltaPayload.trace\_id links delta to the originating plan. (6) **Event Bus**: every event payload carries trace\_id for Orchestrator correlation (plan.ready.v1, plan.failed.v1, etc. per SS22.1.3). **Verification**: trace invariant (SS22.1.4) -- enforced by test `assert_trace_id_propagated()` helper from M7 Epic 7.0 which checks ALL adapter captures + ALL events + ALL deltas carry the same trace\_id. **Implementation**: this is NOT new code but verification that trace\_id stamp calls from 8.2.1 are correctly placed in ALL 8 outbound port calls (SS22.1.2 table). Document cross-service trace topology. |

> **Wiring**: tracing.py [F10] provides helper functions imported by PipelineController and Stage Services. Trace propagation is wired at the SAME points where port calls occur -- `stamp_trace_id()` before LLM calls, `trace_id` parameter in tool/state/bridge calls, `trace_id` field in delta/event payloads. No new ports or adapters needed.
>
> **Anti-hallucination**: The Planner NEVER generates trace IDs (SS22.1.1). It NEVER calls `uuid4()` for trace\_id. `stamp_trace_id()` always copies from PlanRequest.trace\_id. Span creation is best-effort (no pipeline impact if tracing unavailable). Cross-service trace linking is through existing port call parameters, not through a separate tracing bus.
>
> **Dependencies**: M1 (types -- StageContext.trace\_id, DeltaPayload.trace\_id), M2 (PipelineController -- stage orchestration points), M3 (stage services -- LLM call points), M4 (ToolCallRouter -- tool call points), M6 (adapters -- port call points). K1 telemetry subsystem (external -- span API). **Estimated files**: 1 new (tracing.py), 6 modified (pipeline\_controller.py, sketch\_service.py, expand\_service.py, validate\_service.py, tool\_call\_router.py, hil\_coordinator.py).

> - [ ] Span hierarchy: `planner.pipeline` (parent) -> `planner.SKETCH` -> `planner.EXPAND` -> `planner.VALIDATE` -> `planner.COMMIT` (children)
> - [ ] Span creation is best-effort: if K1 telemetry unavailable, span functions are no-ops (no pipeline impact)

### Epic 8.3: Structured Logging

**Goal**: Implement the 34 structured log events cataloged in planner.md SS22.2.1. Every log is a structured JSON record with mandatory fields (`trace_id`, `timestamp_ms`, `level`, `component`). No free-form string logs. All sensitive data handling follows SS22.2.3 (truncation, field omission, no raw prompts).

> **Reference**: planner.md SS22.2 (structured logs), SS22.2.1 (34-event log catalog with level, emitter, timing, key fields), SS22.2.2 (log level policy: DEBUG/INFO/WARN/ERROR), SS22.2.3 (sensitive data handling -- 5 rules), SS22.5 (error recovery observability intensification).
>
> - **Log event naming**: dot-separated (`plan.received`, `stage.started`, `llm.call_completed`). All events structured JSON.
> - **Component field**: always `"planner"` (module-level constant).
> - **Emitter assignment**: PlannerAgent (plan lifecycle), PipelineController (stage lifecycle + recovery), Stage Services (LLM/tool calls), HILCoordinator (HIL events), CommitService (commit events).
> - **Privacy enforcement**: raw user intent truncated to 80 chars, SessionState contents never logged, LLM prompts never logged, HIL user responses never logged, PlanStep params never logged (SS22.2.3).

Intent truncated to 80 chars (SS22.2.3 privacy). (2) `plan.lock_acquired` (DEBUG): emitted at `_plan_lock.acquire()` with `{request_id}`.
