(7) `commit.event_published` (INFO): after plan.ready.v1 published with `{request_id, plan_id, publish_latency_ms}`. (8) `commit.event_failed` (ERROR): after plan.ready event publish failure with `{request_id, plan_id, error_detail}`. This is the ONLY ERROR-level log in the Planner -- plan completed but undeliverable. **Recovery logs** (from 8.3.1): `recovery.fallback` and `recovery.auto_approve` are also error classification logs. `recovery.auto_approve` (WARN): emitted by ValidateService when arbiter unavailable and deterministic checks passed with `{request_id, deterministic_only: true}`. **Privacy**: `error_detail` is exception message only (no stack traces in structured logs -- stack traces go to DEBUG-level separate logger if needed). `failure_detail` in validation logs describes the check failure (e.g., "cycle detected between s1 and s2"), not plan content. |
| 8.3.4 | HIL interaction logs | TODO | k1/planner/services/hil\_coordinator.py [F26] | **6 HIL-related log events** from SS22.2.1. **Clarification logs** (3): (1) `hil.clarification_sent` (INFO): emitted when clarification question published with `{request_id, plan_id, round}` where `round` is 1 or 2. Note: question TEXT is NOT logged (SS22.2.3 -- no HIL content in logs). Only metadata. (2) `hil.clarification_received` (INFO): emitted when user response received with `{request_id, plan_id, round, latency_ms}`. `latency_ms` = time between sent and received. Note: response CONTENT is NOT logged -- only `response_type` and latency. (3) `hil.clarification_timeout` (WARN): emitted on 60s timeout with `{request_id, plan_id, round}`. Indicates best-effort proceed path activated (SS19.7 ERR\_HIL\_TIMEOUT). **Approval logs** (3): (4) `hil.approval_sent` (INFO): emitted when approval request published with `{request_id, plan_id, step_count, has_high_impact}`. `has_high_impact` boolean indicates whether plan contains high-risk side effects (drives timeout behavior). (5) `hil.approval_received` (INFO): emitted when user response received with `{request_id, plan_id, response_type, latency_ms}` where `response_type` in {approved, rejected, modified}. Note: modification DETAILS are NOT logged. (6) `hil.approval_timeout` (WARN): emitted on 120s timeout with `{request_id, plan_id, auto_approved}` where `auto_approved` boolean indicates whether plan was auto-approved (safe plan, no high-impact) or failed (risky plan). **Privacy enforcement** (SS22.2.3): HIL logs MUST NOT contain question text, user response content, plan summary text, or side effect descriptions. Log only: request\_id, plan\_id, round number, response\_type, latency\_ms, boolean flags. This is explicitly required by SS22.2.3 rule 4: "HIL user responses (log response\_type and latency only)". |

> **Wiring**: structured log calls are added to existing service methods at the defined emission points (SS22.2.1 "When" column). All calls go through `tracing.log_event()` from tracing.py [F10] (8.2.1). Log records are JSON-structured with mandatory fields (`trace_id`, `timestamp_ms`, `level`, `component`).
>
> **Anti-hallucination**: No free-form string logs. Every log is a named structured event with defined key fields. No FATAL/CRITICAL level exists -- PlannerAgent never crashes (SS20.6, Principle 3). commit.event\_failed is the only ERROR-level log. Privacy rules from SS22.2.3 are mandatory: intent truncated to 80 chars, no raw prompts, no SessionState data, no HIL content, no PlanStep params.
>
> **Dependencies**: M2 (PipelineController), M3 (stage services), M4 (HILCoordinator), Epic 8.2 (tracing.py), K1 telemetry logger (external). **Estimated files**: 8 modified (planner\_agent.py, pipeline\_controller.py, 4 stage services, hil\_coordinator.py, commit\_service.py).

> - [ ] Every log record is JSON-structured with mandatory fields: `trace_id`, `timestamp_ms`, `level`, `component="planner"`
> - [ ] Log levels follow SS22.2.2 policy: DEBUG (internal state), INFO (lifecycle/completion), WARN (degradation/timeout), ERROR (only `commit.event_failed`)
> - [ ] Privacy enforcement per SS22.2.3: user intent truncated to 80 chars, no raw prompts, no SessionState data, no HIL content, no PlanStep params

> - [ ] 6 LLM call logs emitted by stage services (llm.call_started, llm.call_completed, llm.call_failed) -- log token COUNTS, never prompt text
> - [ ] 8 error/recovery logs emitted by PipelineController + ValidateService + CommitService
> - [ ] 6 HIL interaction logs emitted by HILCoordinator -- log only metadata (round, latency_ms, response_type), NEVER content
> - [ ] No FATAL/CRITICAL level exists -- PlannerAgent never crashes (SS20.6 Principle 3)

> - [ ] `ruff check` passes on all modified files

### Epic 8.4: Delta Emissions

**Goal**: Implement the 11 delta type emissions via IDeltaEmitPort, integrated into PipelineController as the sole delta emitter (SS17.2). Deltas are fire-and-forget cognitive signals for the Learning Loop, SessionState subscribers, and debugging tools. Delta failure NEVER blocks the pipeline.

> **Reference**: planner.md SS22.4 (delta emissions), SS22.4.1 (DeltaPayload schema), SS22.4.2 (11 delta type catalog), SS22.4.3 (15 emission points in pipeline flow), SS22.4.4 (delta vs log vs event comparison), SS15.7 (IDeltaEmitPort spec), SS16.1.6 (DeltaBusAdapter), SS17.2 (PipelineController as sole delta emitter).
>
> - **Sole emitter**: PipelineController is the ONLY component that calls `IDeltaEmitPort.emit()`. Stage services, ToolCallRouter, and HILCoordinator report their results to PipelineController, which then emits the corresponding delta. This centralizes emission logic.
> - **DeltaPayload**: `DeltaPayload(agent_id="planner", delta_type, section, data, trace_id)`. `agent_id` is always "planner" (pre-bound by DeltaBusAdapter).
> - **Topic**: all deltas published to `k1.planner.delta.v1`.
> - **Fire-and-forget contract**: `IDeltaEmitPort.emit()` MUST NOT block, MUST NOT raise. Adapter catches all errors internally.

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 8.4.1 | 11 delta type implementations | TODO | k1/planner/pipeline\_controller.py [F03] | **11 delta type emission implementations** (SS22.4.2) in PipelineController. Each delta is a `DeltaPayload(agent_id="planner", delta_type, section, data, trace_id)` emitted via `_delta_port.emit()`. **stage\_transition deltas** (3 variants, ~8 emission points): (1) Stage start: `DeltaPayload(delta_type="stage_transition", section="pipeline", data={"stage": "SKETCH", "status": "started", "timestamp_ms": now()}, trace_id)`. Emitted at: SKETCH start, EXPAND start, VALIDATE start, COMMIT start (+ micro variants). (2) Stage complete: `data={"stage": ..., "status": "completed", "tokens_used": N, "duration_ms": N}`. Emitted at: each stage completion. (3) Stage failed: `data={"stage": ..., "status": "failed", "error_type": "...", "recovery_path": "ERR_..."}`. Emitted at: each stage failure. **tool\_result delta** (1 type, up to 6 emission points): `DeltaPayload(delta_type="tool_result", section="tools", data={"tool": "discover_capabilities", "result_count": N, "latency_ms": N})`. Emitted after each discovery tool call returns. ToolCallRouter reports result to PipelineController, which emits the delta. **hil\_event deltas** (2 variants): (1) HIL round start: `data={"type": "clarification"/"approval", "round": 1, "plan_id": "..."}`. (2) HIL round end: `data={"type": ..., "round": ..., "outcome": "responded"/"timeout"/"auto_approved", "latency_ms": N}`. HILCoordinator reports to PipelineController, which emits. **plan\_update deltas** (3 variants): (1) Plan committed: `data={"plan_id": "...", "step_count": N, "total_tokens": N, "status": "committed"}`. Emitted by CommitService via PipelineController after CommittedPlan assembly. (2) Plan failed: `data={"request_id": "...", "stage": "...", "reason": "...", "status": "failed"}`. (3) Plan cancelled: `data={"request_id": "...", "reason": "...", "status": "cancelled"}`. **plan\_end delta** (1 type): `data={"plan_id": "...", "total_tokens": N, "total_duration_ms": N, "outcome": "ready"/"failed"/"cancelled"}`. Emitted at PLAN\_END (SS23.4 step 4). **micro\_replan delta** (1 type): `data={"original_plan_id": "...", "outcome": "success"/"failed", "replacement_steps": N, "duration_ms": N}`. Emitted at micro-replan completion. **Total**: 11 delta types, ~15 emission points across the pipeline (SS22.4.3). |
| 8.4.2 | Delta Bus integration via IDeltaEmitPort | TODO | k1/planner/pipeline\_controller.py [F03] + k1/planner/adapters/delta\_bus\_adapter.py [F33] | **Delta emission wiring through IDeltaEmitPort** (SS15.7, SS16.1.6). PipelineController holds `_delta_port: IDeltaEmitPort` (injected by factory, 10-step wiring step 7). **Emission method in PipelineController**: `def _emit_delta(self, delta_type: str, section: str, data: Dict[str, Any]) -> None` -- constructs `DeltaPayload(agent_id="planner", delta_type=delta_type, section=section, data=data, trace_id=self._trace_id)` and calls `self._delta_port.emit(delta)`. Synchronous, never async. Wrapped in try/except that catches ALL exceptions and logs warning (fire-and-forget per SS15.7). **DeltaBusAdapter** (already implemented in M6 6.2.6): production adapter that publishes to `k1.planner.delta.v1` topic via `IDeltaBusPort`. Pre-binds `agent_id="planner"` at construction. `emit()` is synchronous, thread-safe, catches all errors internally. **TestDeltaAdapter** (already implemented in M6 6.1.6): test adapter that appends to `_capture: List[DeltaPayload]` for assertion. Used by M7 tests to verify delta emission correctness. **Emission point placement**: PipelineController calls `_emit_delta()` at each of the 15 emission points cataloged in SS22.4.3 (pipeline flow with delta markers). **Ordering guarantee**: deltas are emitted in pipeline execution order (SKETCH start -> SKETCH tool results -> SKETCH complete -> EXPAND start -> ...). No reordering, no batching. Each emission is immediate at the execution point. **Delivery guarantee**: fire-and-forget. Missing deltas are acceptable (SS22.4.4). Delta Bus subscribers (Learning Loop, SessionState) handle gaps gracefully. |
| 8.4.3 | Delta rate limiting | TODO | k1/planner/pipeline\_controller.py [F03] | **Delta rate limiting design** (pragmatic V1 approach). V1 does NOT implement active rate limiting -- with ~15 deltas per plan and max 1 concurrent plan, the emission rate is inherently bounded (~1-2 deltas/second during pipeline execution). **V1 bounds analysis**: Full plan emits ~15 deltas (4 stage starts + 4 stage completes + up to 6 tool results + 1 plan\_update + 1 plan\_end ≈ 16 max). At 12s P50 total pipeline, that is ~1.3 deltas/s. Micro-replan: ~10 deltas in ~8s ≈ 1.25 deltas/s. Under mailbox load (5 queued plans processed serially): 5 x 16 deltas over ~60s = ~1.3 deltas/s sustained. No rate limiting needed at these rates. **V2 consideration**: if V2 multi-plan concurrency (SS24.4) is implemented, delta rate could increase to ~4 deltas/s (3 concurrent plans). Rate limiting via token bucket may be warranted. Decision deferred to V2 ADR. **Defensive measures (V1)**: (1) Delta emission is synchronous and non-blocking -- if DeltaBusAdapter falls behind, deltas are dropped (fire-and-forget). (2) TestDeltaAdapter captures all deltas for assertion -- no rate limit in test mode. (3) PipelineController emits deltas ONLY at defined emission points (SS22.4.3) -- no ad-hoc emission from service code. (4) `_emit_delta()` wrapper catches all exceptions -- even if emission hangs (should not happen with fire-and-forget adapter), the pipeline continues. **Configuration**: `PlannerConfig.delta_rate_limit_per_second: Optional[int] = None` -- None means no limit (V1 default). V2 may set to 10 or 20 per second per plan. **Implementation**: if `delta_rate_limit_per_second` is not None, `_emit_delta()` checks a simple token bucket before emitting. If bucket empty, delta is dropped (logged at DEBUG). Token bucket refills at configured rate. V1: this code path is never activated (config default None). |

> **Wiring**: Delta emissions are centralized in PipelineController (SS17.2 "sole delta emitter"). PipelineController holds `_delta_port: IDeltaEmitPort` from factory injection. The `_emit_delta()` helper method constructs DeltaPayload and calls `_delta_port.emit()` with fire-and-forget semantics. Stage services, ToolCallRouter, and HILCoordinator report results to PipelineController, which emits deltas on their behalf.
>
> **Anti-hallucination**: Only PipelineController calls `_delta_port.emit()`. Stage services do NOT emit deltas directly. Delta emission is synchronous (NOT async). Delta failure is NEVER propagated (fire-and-forget). `agent_id` is always "planner" (pre-bound by DeltaBusAdapter in production, hardcoded in PipelineController for test). V1 rate limiting is NOT active (inherently bounded at ~1.3 deltas/s).
>
> **Dependencies**: M1 (DeltaPayload type), M2 (PipelineController), M6 (IDeltaEmitPort, DeltaBusAdapter, TestDeltaAdapter). **Estimated files**: 1 modified (pipeline\_controller.py -- add `_emit_delta()` method + 15 emission point calls).

> **Done When (Epic 8.4)**:

> - [ ] `_emit_delta()` helper method constructs `DeltaPayload(agent_id="planner", delta_type, section, data, trace_id)` and calls `_delta_port.emit()`
> - [ ] `_emit_delta()` wrapped in try/except that catches ALL exceptions (fire-and-forget per SS15.7)
> - [ ] All 11 delta types implemented: 3 stage_transition variants, 1 tool_result, 2 hil_event variants, 3 plan_update variants, 1 plan_end, 1 micro_replan

> - [ ] Stage services, ToolCallRouter, HILCoordinator report results to PipelineController -- they do NOT call `_delta_port.emit()` directly
> - [ ] Delta emission is synchronous (NOT async); delta failure NEVER propagated

## Milestone 9: Production Readiness

> **Goal**: Validate performance, resilience, documentation, and architecture sync before production deployment. M9 is the final gate -- it does NOT add new features. It validates that all M1-M8 code meets performance targets (SS21), survives chaos scenarios (SS19-20), has correct documentation, and passes governance sync. No issue in M9 is blocked by M9-internal dependencies; all issues can be parallelized.

### Epic 9.1: Performance Benchmarks

**Goal**: Validate that the Planner meets all latency targets from planner.md SS21 using the real M7 test harness (TestLLMAdapter, TestFabricAdapter, etc.) with realistic latency simulation. Benchmarks use `time.monotonic()` wall-clock measurements and assert P50/P99 percentiles over N>=100 runs.

> **Reference**: planner.md SS21.1 (Planner-owned latencies), SS21.2 (resource budgets), SS22.3.2 (histogram bucket definitions), SS22.3.4 (SLI alignment table -- 8 SLIs with P50/P99 targets).
>
> - **Benchmark harness**: Uses `PlannerFactory.create_for_testing()` with test adapters configured with realistic latency simulation (TestLLMAdapter with configurable `response_delay_ms`, TestFabricAdapter with configurable `discovery_delay_ms`). Actual wall-clock time is measured, NOT simulated delays.
> - **Target values** (SS21.1): SKETCH P50 4s / P99 8s, EXPAND P50 3s / P99 5s, VALIDATE P50 2s / P99 3s, COMMIT P50 20ms / P99 100ms, Total P50 12s / P99 30s, Micro-Replan P50 8s / P99 15s.
> - **Benchmark output**: JSON report with per-stage and total P50/P99/P99.9 values, histogram data, and pass/fail status per SLI.

|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|

Runs N>=100 full pipelines (SKETCH -> EXPAND -> VALIDATE -> COMMIT) through `PlannerFactory.create_for_testing()` with realistic TestLLMAdapter latency configuration: SKETCH delay ~3-4s (simulating 2K token generation), EXPAND delay ~2-3s (simulating 1K token generation), VALIDATE delay ~1.5-2s (simulating 500 token generation). TestFabricAdapter configured with ~20-40ms per discovery call (realistic Fabric Retrieval). **Measurements**: wall-clock `time.monotonic()` from `PipelineController.execute()` start to PLAN\_END. Collect P50, P99, P99.9, min, max, stddev. **Assertions**: P50 <= 12s, P99 <= 30s. **Also measures**: plan delivery latency (time from CommitService producing CommittedPlan to plan.ready.v1 event published) -- target P50 2ms / P99 10ms. Token budget utilization: assert total\_tokens <= 3500 (PLAN-11) for every run. **Micro-replan benchmark**: separate N>=50 runs of `micro_replan()` path. > **Anti-hallucination**: Benchmark targets come directly from planner.md SS21.1 table. Benchmarks do NOT simulate delays via `asyncio.sleep()` in the pipeline code -- delays are injected only in test adapters (which represent external service latency). Memory thresholds are initial estimates to be calibrated. Token budgets are contractual (PLAN-05, PLAN-10, PLAN-11).
>
> **Dependencies**: M2 (PipelineController), M3 (stage services), M4 (support services), M6 (adapters), M7 (test harness, test adapters, conftest.py, PlannerFactory.create\_for\_testing). M8 (metrics -- 9.1.4 validates gauge accuracy).

### Epic 9.2: Chaos Testing

**Goal**: Validate that the Planner survives every failure mode described in planner.md SS19 (Error Recovery) and SS20 (Circuit Breakers & Fault Tolerance) without actor crash, deadlock, or resource leak. Chaos tests inject failures at specific points in the pipeline and assert that the Planner follows its defined recovery paths.

> **Reference**: planner.md SS19 (Error Recovery -- 6 named paths), SS19.10 (degradation severity ranking), SS19.11 (error recovery wiring summary), SS20.4 (fault tolerance by port -- 7 ports), SS20.5 (fire-and-forget resilience pattern), SS20.6 (resilience design philosophy -- 3 principles), SS23.5 (SHUTDOWN -- 8-step sequence), SS24.2 (cancellation -- 5 checkpoints).
>
> - **Chaos injection mechanism**: Test adapters raise configured exceptions at configured call counts. E.g., `TestLLMAdapter.set_failure(stage="SKETCH", call_index=0, error=LLMTimeoutError())` causes the first SKETCH LLM call to timeout.
> - **Principle under test**: "Degrade, Never Crash" (SS20.6 Principle 1). Every chaos test asserts the PlannerAgent remains alive and mailbox-ready after the injected failure.
> - **Assertion pattern**: (1) Expected recovery path activated (counter/delta). (2) Correct terminal event emitted (plan.ready, plan.failed, or plan.cancelled). (3) PlannerAgent processes next plan normally (actor survival). (4) No resource leak (plan\_lock released, counters reset, mailbox accepting).

| Issue | Title | Status | Deliverable | Details |
|-------|-------|--------|-------------|---------|
| 9.2.1 | LLM timeout scenarios | TODO | tests/k1/planner/chaos/test\_llm\_chaos.py | **LLM failure injection across all 3 stages** (SS19, SS20.3). Uses TestLLMAdapter's failure injection API. **SKETCH LLM timeout** (ERR\_SKETCH\_FAIL path, SS19.1): (1) First call times out -> retry with simplified prompt (TestLLMAdapter failure on call 0, success on call 1). Assert: `recovery_activated{path=ERR_SKETCH_FAIL}` counter == 1, `stage_total{stage=SKETCH, outcome=retry}` counter == 1, plan completes normally. (2) Both calls timeout -> plan.failed.v1 emitted. Assert: `plan_total{outcome=failed}` == 1, PlannerAgent processes next plan normally (actor survival). **EXPAND LLM timeout** (ERR\_EXPAND\_FAIL path, SS19.2): (1) First call times out -> retry. Success on retry -> plan continues. (2) Both calls fail -> fallback to SKETCH output as degraded plan. Assert: `plan_degraded{stage=EXPAND}` == 1, plan completes as degraded (plan.ready.v1 emitted with degraded flag). **VALIDATE LLM timeout** (ERR\_VALIDATE\_FAIL path, SS19.3): (1) Arbiter timeout -> auto-approve if deterministic checks passed. Assert: `recovery_activated{path=ERR_VALIDATE_FAIL}` == 1, structured log `recovery.auto_approve` emitted. (2) Arbiter timeout + deterministic checks failed -> retry once with EXPAND re-run. **Sustained failure** (SS20.3.3): all 3 LLM calls in sequence fail -> plan.failed.v1. Assert: all recovery paths activated in sequence, actor survives, mailbox intact. **Budget exceeded**: TestLLMAdapter returns `BudgetExceededError` -> immediate plan.failed.v1 (no retry per SS20.3.2). **Total scenarios**: ~12 chaos tests. |

No pipeline started. **ILLMPort** (covered by 9.2.1 -- cross-reference only). **IFabricRetrievalPort** (SS20.4 row 3): (1) `discover_capabilities()` raises exception -> ToolCallRouter returns empty `RetrievalResult`. Assert: `tool_calls{tool=discover_capabilities, outcome=timeout}` == 1, pipeline continues with reduced capability list (degraded quality, not failure). (2) `find_relevant_prompts()` raises -> empty result, pipeline continues. (3) Both discovery tools fail -> SketchService proceeds with zero capabilities (llm\_prompt slots empty for tools). Plan may be low quality but NOT failed. **IStateReadPort** (SS20.4 row 4): `read_sections()` raises -> empty dict returned. Assert: ToolCallRouter degrades, pipeline continues. **IBridgePort** (SS20.4 row 5): (1) `recall()` raises -> empty RecallResponse. Pipeline continues (degraded context). (2) `persist_plan()` raises -> log warning, retry once. On second failure: plan.ready.v1 still emitted (durability loss, not correctness loss per SS9.3.2). (3) Both recall + persist fail -> plan still completes (fire-and-forget WAL). **IDeltaEmitPort** (SS20.4 row 6): `emit()` raises -> silently caught by `_emit_delta()` wrapper. Assert: pipeline completes normally, no delta emitted (fire-and-forget). Zero impact on plan outcome. **IEventPort** (SS20.4 row 7): (1) `publish()` raises on plan.ready.v1 -> retry once. On second failure: plan completed but undeliverable (commit.event\_failed ERROR log). This is the highest-severity port failure. (2) `subscribe()` raises during INIT -> PlannerAgent cannot start (fatal at startup only). **Actor survival check**: after every port failure scenario, enqueue and process one more plan to verify PlannerAgent is still alive and functional. **Total scenarios**: ~14 chaos tests (2 per port avg). |
| 9.2.3 | Cancel during each pipeline stage | TODO | tests/k1/planner/chaos/test\_cancel\_chaos.py | **Cancellation at every defined checkpoint** (SS24.2.2 -- 5 cancel checkpoints). Uses `MailboxAdapter.send_cancel(request_id)` injected at precise timing points. **Checkpoint 1: Pre-execution cancel** (PLAN\_START step 2): cancel arrives before plan dequeued. Assert: plan.cancelled.v1 emitted with `reason="cancelled_before_start"`, no lock acquired, no stage executed, PlannerAgent immediately processes next queued plan. **Checkpoint 2: Between SKETCH and EXPAND**: TestLLMAdapter configured with a callback that calls `send_cancel()` during SKETCH return processing. Assert: plan.cancelled.v1 with `reason="cancelled_between_stages"` and `stage="SKETCH"`, EXPAND never started, plan\_lock released, counters reset. **Checkpoint 3: Between EXPAND and VALIDATE**: same pattern, cancel injected at EXPAND->VALIDATE boundary. Assert: `stage="EXPAND"`, VALIDATE never started. **Checkpoint 4: Between VALIDATE and COMMIT**: cancel injected at VALIDATE->COMMIT boundary. Assert: `stage="VALIDATE"`, COMMIT (WAL persist, event publish) never executed. **Checkpoint 5: Before WAL persist** (CommitService): cancel injected just before `IBridgePort.persist_plan()`. Assert: plan.cancelled.v1 emitted, WAL NOT persisted, plan.ready.v1 NOT published. **Edge cases** (SS24.2.4): (1) Cancel after COMPLETED (plan.ready.v1 already emitted) -> no-op. Assert: no plan.cancelled.v1 emitted, plan.ready.v1 already delivered. (2) Cancel for unknown request\_id -> silent no-op (added to \_cancel\_set, never matched). (3) Double cancel (same request\_id) -> idempotent. (4) Cancel arrives before enqueue -> request\_id pre-loaded in \_cancel\_set, immediate cancel when dequeued. **Resource leak check**: after every cancel scenario, verify plan\_lock is released, stage\_token\_usage cleared, tool\_call\_count == 0, hil\_round\_count == 0. Process another plan to confirm. **Total scenarios**: ~10 chaos tests. |
| 9.2.4 | Micro-replan failure during main pipeline | TODO | tests/k1/planner/chaos/test\_micro\_replan\_chaos.py | **Micro-replan failure modes** (SS10.6, SS24.3). **Lock contention** (SS24.3.2): micro\_replan() called while a full plan is in flight. Assert: micro\_replan awaits \_plan\_lock but caller times out at 10s (V1 behavior). `micro_replan()` returns None. Orchestrator falls back to original plan. Main plan completes normally (no interference). **Micro-replan SKETCH fail**: TestLLMAdapter configured to fail on micro-SKETCH LLM call. Assert: all-failures-return-None simplification (SS10.6). No plan.failed.v1 emitted (micro-replan failure is silent return None, not event emission). **Micro-replan EXPAND fail**: LLM failure during micro-EXPAND. Same: returns None. **Micro-replan VALIDATE fail**: LLM arbiter failure. Same: returns None (SS10.6 simplification -- all micro-replan failures collapse to None). **Micro-replan token budget exceeded**: micro-replan total tokens exceed 2000 budget. Assert: budget enforcement, returns None. **Micro-replan with cancel**: cancel flag set during micro-replan execution. Assert: micro-replan returns None (not plan.cancelled.v1 -- cancel for micro-replan is silent). Main plan is NOT affected by micro-replan cancel. **Sequential micro-replan**: 3 sequential micro-replan calls (from different DAG failures). Assert: each runs independently, state fully cleared between runs. No counter drift, no lock leak. **Overlap detection test**: micro-replan with discoveries that overlap remaining steps (SS10.2.2 heuristic). Assert: overlap detected, affected steps included in re-scope. **Total scenarios**: ~10 chaos tests. |

> **Wiring**: Chaos tests use `PlannerFactory.create_for_testing()` with test adapters configured to inject failures at specific call indices. Failure injection is configured BEFORE the pipeline runs -- no runtime monkey-patching.
>
> **Anti-hallucination**: Every chaos scenario maps to a specific error recovery path in SS19 or fault tolerance row in SS20.4. No invented failure modes. Cancel checkpoints are the exact 5 from SS24.2.2. Micro-replan all-failures-return-None is per SS10.6 simplification. The Planner NEVER crashes from any injected failure (SS20.6 Principle 1). COMMIT has no LLM call (PLAN-03), so no LLM chaos test for Stage 4.
>
> **Dependencies**: M2 (PipelineController), M3 (stage services), M4 (support services), M5 (error recovery), M6 (adapters with failure injection APIs), M7 (test harness). **Estimated scenarios**: ~46 chaos tests total across 4 files.

> **Done When (Epic 9.2)**:
>
> - [ ] Failure injected via test adapter configuration BEFORE pipeline runs (no runtime monkey-patching)
> - [ ] ~12 LLM chaos tests: timeout per stage (retry + fail), sustained failure, budget exceeded (SS19, SS20.3)
> - [ ] ~14 port chaos tests: each of 7 ports with injected failures per SS20.4 fault tolerance table
> - [ ] ~10 cancel chaos tests: 5 cancel checkpoints from SS24.2.2 + 4 edge cases (post-complete, unknown id, double cancel, pre-enqueue)
> - [ ] ~10 micro-replan chaos tests: lock contention, per-stage failures, budget exceeded, cancel during micro-replan, sequential runs (SS10.6, SS24.3)
> - [ ] Every chaos scenario maps to a named recovery path in SS19 or fault tolerance row in SS20.4 -- no invented failure modes
> - [ ] After every failure injection, PlannerAgent processes next plan normally (actor survival: "Degrade, Never Crash" per SS20.6 Principle 1)
> - [ ] No resource leak: `plan_lock` released, `stage_token_usage` cleared, `tool_call_count == 0`, `hil_round_count == 0`, mailbox accepting
> - [ ] Correct terminal event emitted for every chaos scenario: `plan.ready.v1`, `plan.failed.v1`, or `plan.cancelled.v1`
> - [ ] `ruff check` passes on all chaos test files

### Epic 9.3: Documentation

**Goal**: Create the minimum required documentation for production operation: module README (architecture overview + API surface), pipeline troubleshooting runbook, and port/service API reference. Documentation is generated from the implemented code and planner.md -- no speculative content.

> **Reference**: planner.md SS30 (Directory & Implementation Guide), SS25 (External Touchpoints), SS26 (Delta Bus & Event Bus), SS30.7 (Port-to-Service Assignment Matrix), SS30.8 (Contract File Locations), SS30.9 (Implementation Priorities).
>
> - **Documentation standard** (copilot-instructions.md): "No unrequested documentation." M9 documentation is explicitly requested as part of production readiness. Minimal, factual, code-derived.
> - **README scope**: architecture overview, directory structure, port inventory, service inventory, configuration reference, event catalog, quick start (how to instantiate and test).
> - **Runbook scope**: per-error-path troubleshooting, metric alert thresholds, delta interpretation, structured log event reference.

Structure: **Section 1: Failure Triage Decision Tree** -- start with `plan.failed.v1` event, branch by `error_code` field: ERR\_SKETCH\_FAIL -> check LLM availability (Model Hub health), ERR\_EXPAND\_FAIL -> check discovery tool responses (Fabric Retrieval health), ERR\_VALIDATE\_FAIL -> check LLM arbiter (may need prompt tuning), ERR\_COMMIT\_FAIL -> check K0 Bridge + Event Bus, ERR\_HIL\_TIMEOUT -> check Concierge relay (user may be offline). **Section 2: Metric Alert Thresholds** -- recommended alert rules: `plan_total{outcome=failed}` rate > 3/min (indicates systemic issue), `stage_duration_ms{stage=SKETCH}` P99 > 8s (LLM degradation), `mailbox_rejected` rate > 1/min (overload -- consider scaling), `recovery_activated` rate > 5/min (cascading failures). **Section 3: Structured Log Reference** -- 34 log events from SS22.2.1 with grep patterns, example JSON, and interpretation (e.g., `commit.event_failed` is ERROR level -- plan completed but undeliverable, check Event Bus). **Section 4: Delta Interpretation** -- 11 delta types from SS22.4.2 with payload examples and consumer guidance (Learning Loop vs debugging). **Section 5: Common Failure Scenarios** -- 10 scenarios from SS5.6 (pipeline flow failure table) with step-by-step diagnosis: which logs to check, which metrics to correlate, which recovery path was taken, expected system behavior. **Section 6: Circuit Breaker Impact** -- CB\_PLANNER trip symptoms (all plans rejected, mailbox\_rejected counter spikes), CB\_LLM\_INHERIT symptoms (LLM-dependent stages fail, recovery paths activate). Note: Planner does NOT own CBs (SS20.6 Principle 2) -- diagnosis requires Orchestrator and Model Hub logs. |
